# BAGI Autonomous Research Log

## 2026-08-16 — Oracle search ceiling diagnostic

- Hypothesis: the Workshop v0.2 planner failure may come from random-shooting search rather than from learned-model rollout error.
- Evidence entering this cycle: the 50-guided learned planner reaches the switch substantially more often than it crosses the door, and increasing horizon from 5 to 8 did not restore success. The existing hourly research workflow was still rerunning the older sample-efficiency study rather than the current planner diagnostic.
- Change: added `bagi/experiments/oracle_search_ceiling.py`, `configs/oracle_search_ceiling.yaml`, and the `make oracle-search-ceiling` target. The diagnostic replaces the learned world model with exact Workshop v0.2 transition dynamics while preserving the same random-shooting MPC structure, horizon, discount, event weights, candidate counts, and planner seeds.
- Result: implementation is committed; a repository-run result has not yet been observed in this cycle, so no oracle-success claim is recorded yet.
- Next measurement: run `make check && make oracle-search-ceiling`. If oracle planning succeeds while learned planning fails under the same search budget, compare learned-vs-oracle candidate sequence scores/event predictions to localize rollout calibration error. If oracle planning also fails, focus on search/scoring itself.

## 2026-10-21 — E7 event-label-stratified calibration diagnostic

- Hypothesis: aggregate event Brier scores on planner-selected trajectories may conceal errors on rare event-positive labels. This is a measurement hypothesis, not a claim that positive-event calibration is poor.
- Prior evidence: the 2026-10-20 BAGI Research Loop artifact (run 39507615949) reports planner whole-observation accuracy 0.139 at horizon 1 and 0.028 at horizon 8, with 0 goal/lock/key events across three planner episodes. At horizon 1, only 2 of 144 evaluated event labels are positive, while the aggregate event Brier is 0.000294. The 128/512/2048/4096 candidate sweep does not improve causal-stage completion, whereas the exact-state oracle succeeds at 128/512/2048 candidates. This does not isolate partial observability or prove model exploitation.
- Change: extend the existing E6 offline diagnostic to report pooled positive-label and negative-label event Brier scores, squared-error sums, and label counts at each horizon and policy. Weight by actual label counts across anchors and episodes, rather than averaging per-episode positive Brier scores; represent zero-positive-label Brier as null. Add deterministic unit tests for rare positives and no-positive cases. Retain original metrics and fixed evaluation seeds (50042 onward); no extra training interactions or new architecture.
- Status: code proposed for CI; no new E7 metric has been measured in this entry. The fixed Workshop layout, reused checkpoint, and on-policy trajectory anchors remain limitations.
- Next measurement: run the imagination-gap diagnostic with the cached causal-v2 checkpoint and compare positive vs negative Brier and label counts for guided, random, and planner trajectories at horizons 1, 3, 5, 8. If positive labels remain sparse, report insufficient evidence rather than attributing failure to positive-event miscalibration.

## 2026-10-21 — E8 matched teacher-forcing versus recursive rollout diagnostic

- Hypothesis: the learned model's poor planner-selected long-horizon predictions may reflect compounding errors from feeding its own predicted observations back into the model, rather than solely poor one-step predictions on the real states visited by the planner. This is a diagnostic hypothesis, not a causal conclusion.
- Evidence: the completed E7 imagination-gap artifact (run 39523338422; independently repeated in research run 39528684341) reports planner whole-observation accuracy 0.139 at horizon 1, 0.056 at horizon 5, and 0.028 at horizon 8 (36 comparisons per horizon); guided accuracy is 1.0 at horizons 5 and 8, but guided/planner states are not matched. Planner negative-label Brier is 0.019435 at horizon 5 with zero positive labels at that depth. Copilot's latest workflow (39529321501) completed without changing the research branch.
- Change: within each existing real trajectory anchor and evaluation depth, compute a one-step teacher-forced prediction from the actual observation immediately before that step using the identical recorded action and actual next observation. Report teacher_forced_cell_accuracy, teacher_forced_whole_observation_accuracy, teacher_forced_reward_mae, and teacher_forced_event_brier alongside the original recursively imagined metrics. Add a deterministic test where recursive and teacher-forced predictions diverge despite identical actions and targets. Preserve all original E7 metrics, policy episodes, seeds (50042 onward), model checkpoint, and interaction counts.
- Status: diagnostic implementation submitted for CI and E6 workflow; no E8 measurement is claimed yet.
- Next measurement: compare teacher-forced and recursive accuracy on the same planner, guided, and random transitions at horizons 1, 3, 5, 8. At horizon 1 they should agree; if they separate at longer horizons, quantify compounding error. If both remain poor, investigate one-step errors on planner-visited states before altering search. Fixed layout, limited episodes, and on-policy anchor selection remain limitations.

## 2026-10-21 — E9 uncertainty-penalized planning ablation

- Hypothesis: penalizing low-confidence imagined states may reduce error exploitation and improve stage progression under the same learned model and search budget; this is a falsifiable, not confirmed, improvement claim.
- Evidence: completed E8 run 39531040276 (commit 2e0deea) measured planner whole-observation prediction accuracy of 0.028 recursively versus 0.194 teacher-forced at horizon 8 (36 matched comparisons). Teacher-forced accuracy is also poor, so compounding error is only part of the problem. The agent did not reach the key, lock or goal in those three evaluation episodes.
- Change: add an optional confidence-penalized MPC planner (one minus mean maximum cell probability), preserve the original planner when penalty is zero, and compare weights 0.0/0.25/1.0 at fixed horizon 8, 128 candidates, 3 planner seeds and 3 evaluation episodes per seed. Reuse the existing 50-guided world-model checkpoint; capture causal stage rates, real evaluation steps, imagined steps and paired goal-rate differences. Add deterministic unit tests and run E9 alongside the existing E6 diagnostic on research-branch pushes.
- Limitations: softmax confidence is not calibrated uncertainty; the world layout is fixed, and three seeds cannot establish significance or transfer.
- Status: implementation submitted for GitHub CI and E9 evaluation. No E9 success claim is made before artifact verification.
- Next measurement: compare stage rates and actual real-step counts for each weight. If there is no gain, inspect confidence calibration on planner-selected states before expanding compute or changing architecture.

## 2026-10-21 — E10 held-out replication of uncertainty planning

- Research question: does the E9 improvement at early causal stages for uncertainty_weight=1.0 survive new, previously unused planner seeds at the exact same search budget? Can it advance beyond the door?
- Prior evidence: E9 GitHub run 39540599254 (46e10ed) reported switch activation 5/9 at baseline versus 9/9 at weight 1.0; door reached 0/9 versus 1/9; key, lock and goal were 0/9 in both. E9 was exploratory with only three planner seeds, and the stronger penalty was chosen after examining the E9 arms. It does not justify a capability improvement claim.
- Preregistered primary comparison: baseline weight 0 versus E9-selected weight 1.0, 12 *new* planner seeds [404, 505, 606, 707, 808, 909, 1001, 1102, 1203, 1304, 1405, 1506] and three matched evaluation episodes each. All other Workshop layout, environment seeds, model checkpoint, 128 candidates, horizon 8, event weights and step budgets stay unchanged. Stage rates (switch/door/key/lock/goal), real environment steps, imagined steps and seed-wise paired differences are recorded. Exact two-sided sign-flip probabilities describe variation across planner seeds but are exploratory across five correlated stages and one fixed layout.
- Implementation: add E10 YAML, pure small-sample paired-stage analysis and tests, and call E10 in the existing E6/E9 workflow. No training data recollection or world-model retraining planned.
- Status: E10 implementation proposed, no E10 result known yet. Next: inspect its uploaded summary and require reproducible gains on held-out seeds before testing a new architecture or generalization claim. If the effect vanishes, report the negative result and investigate model confidence calibration or action bias.

### E10 measured result — GitHub Actions run 39541374030

- Validation: CI run 39541374036 and the E6/E9/E10 diagnostic run 39541374030 both succeeded at commit d160d8daff8e5605a1824c1df7aa31000cdce4e3. E10 full JSON is in artifact bagi-e6-e9-e10-research, planner_uncertainty_replication/summary.json.
- Held-out 12 planner seeds × 3 fixed-layout episodes (36 per arm): at penalty 0.0 versus 1.0 respectively, switch 24/36 vs 36/36, door 1/36 vs 5/36, key 1/36 vs 5/36, lock 1/36 vs 0/36, goal 0/36 vs 0/36.
- Paired seed-level mean gain: switch +0.3333, exact sign-flip p=0.0078125 (8 improved seeds, 4 ties); door and key each +0.1111, p=0.125 (4 improved, 8 ties); lock -0.02778, p=1.0; goal 0, p=1.0. These are unadjusted descriptive tests over correlated stages, reused environment seeds, one fixed world layout and one world-model checkpoint. The planner-seed findings alone do not establish transfer or calibrated uncertainty.
- Interpretation: early-stage switch gains replicate under held-out planner randomization. Door/key gains remain inconclusive, and the penalty does not improve full-sequence success. The lock result prevents a claim of general causal-planning improvement.
- E11 candidate: isolate the key-to-lock bottleneck with state/observation visibility and event-conditioned diagnostics after key collection, matching trajectories and avoiding hidden-state inputs to the learned planner. Test whether confidence penalty suppresses predicted lock progression or whether partial-observation memory is necessary; do not tune additional weights on E10 data and call them held-out.

## 2026-10-21 — Gate A: establish training-data diversity and observable-state limits

- Scientific basis: POMDP theory requires a history or belief state when current observations alias hidden situations. Independent validation requires avoiding identical or near-identical training examples in evaluation.
- Prior evidence: Workshop v0.2 resets to the same layout and starting cell on every seed; 50 guided episodes follow the identical action list. The world model trains on an independent random *transition-level* split, not trajectory groups, so duplicated guided outcomes can cross the boundary. E10 improved switch reachability but failed 0/36 goals with either planner, motivating prerequisite checks before new planner weight sweeps.
- Hypothesis: substantial duplication across the actual training/validation split inflates the ease of validation; some same-visible-observation/action groups have distinct outcomes or hidden flags. This is not yet a measured model-accuracy inflation or a claim that ambiguity alone causes planner failure.
- Change: add offline Gate A dataset audit with exact supervised transition keys, guided trajectory fingerprints, positive causal-event counts, the actual deterministic training split, episode-disjoint and identical-trajectory-disjoint counterfactual splits, and observation/action outcome ambiguity flagged separately from hidden-state-flag variation. Unit tests cover duplicates, splits, aliasing and invalid inputs. Run on existing causal-v2 collection in E6/E9/E10 workflow; reconstruct same deterministic 50-guided/50-random collection without training only if cached logs are absent, with provenance explicitly recorded.
- Next measurement: inspect new Gate A JSON artifact, report exact duplicate fractions and positive-event coverage, and decide whether the next step should be an episode/trajectory-disjoint learning baseline, a more diverse policy dataset, or an information-matched memory baseline. Do not make a transfer claim from this fixed layout.

### Gate A measured result — GitHub Actions 39543001910

- Reproducibility: CI 39543001783 and workflow 39543001910 succeeded at commit 7377f306b2f964c4af8d23f0830ced66c362edc9. Read the machine-readable result in artifact \`bagi-e6-e9-e10-gate-a\` at \`dataset_foundations/summary.json\`.
- Dataset provenance: **restored original checkpoint cache**, not reconstructed. Logged 5,765 transitions from 100 episodes (50 guided and 50 random), but only 201 distinct exact supervised (visible observation, action, next observation, reward, four event labels) transition keys. 5,739/5,765 rows occur in duplicated supervised transitions, so these are not independent training samples.
- Guided coverage: 50 guided episodes contain **one unique episode signature** (49 duplicates). Episode count and training transition count are therefore not substitutes for experience diversity.
- Actual training split, exactly mirroring \`train_world_model.py\` seed 42 and validation fraction 0.2: 1,145/1,153 validation transitions (99.306%) exactly match a supervised training transition. A counterfactual episode-disjoint split also overlaps by 1,238/1,244 (99.518%). Even holding out all identical trajectory groups leaves 1,640/1,700 exact validation transition overlaps (96.471%). These quantify evaluation leakage risk, **not** experimentally measured inflation of model accuracy.
- Causal event label support: switch 99, key 62, lock 81, goal 51 positive *rows*, but many come from duplicated guided episodes; counts are not independent coverage. Splitting the same fixed layout by reset seed is not unseen-layout generalization.
- Observation aliasing: 198 unique visible-observation/action keys, 172 repeated; three have more than one recorded outcome (250 rows total); 49 repeated keys have mixed hidden flags, of which two also have differing outcomes. Different hidden flags alone do not establish different next outcomes; additional geometry/trajectory analysis is needed.
- Scientific interpretation: trust in the current random-transition validation metric as evidence of out-of-distribution predictive competence is unjustified. We have a data-diversity and evaluation-design problem before any need for a larger planner or new uncertainty term can be established.
- Next decisive Gate A experiment: replace transition-level model selection with a clearly distinct observable-input/trajectory-level holdout or a genuinely varied initial state/layout, report held-out event-positive accuracy and causal planning separately, and compare at the same training transition budget. Do not claim that trajectory-disjoint alone creates novel states; if introducing new layouts, ensure the held-out layout is not used in training.

## 2026-10-21 — Gate A2: information-matched model evaluation on reachable state/action coverage

- Established principle: POMDP observations can alias multiple hidden states; predictive performance should be measured where a model's observation/action inputs were absent during training, rather than on duplicates from the random transition split.
- Evidence: Gate A audit (run 39543001910) found 5,765 rows but 201 unique supervised transitions, with 99.3% of ordinary validation rows exactly duplicated in the training split. That overlap does not itself quantify predictive error or generalization.
- Hypothesis: one-step next-view and event accuracy for *reachable but unseen* observation/action pairs differs substantially from pairs present in the original training split. A similar result would instead favor exploring longer-horizon compounding error and hidden-state ambiguity rather than blaming only limited observation/action coverage.
- Design: hold the original 50-guided checkpoint and training data fixed. Breadth-first enumerate every reachable non-terminal Workshop state and four actions using real simulator replay, deduplicating positions/key/door flags **for evaluation-set construction only**. Reconstruct exactly the original seeded training split; label each evaluation transition as seen/unseen by its visible observation/action key. Feed **only visible local observation and action** into the original world model; report cell/whole-view accuracy, reward MAE, positive/negative event Brier and actual event-positive support by partition. Count simulator replay steps separately; no new training data or retraining. Unit tests verify deterministic reachability, event coverage, original seeded split, and no privileged inputs to the learned model.
- Limits: the evaluation uses the same fixed world layout and model checkpoint, so it is *not* a cross-layout transfer test. Not all statistically independent samples are available because distinct hidden states can share visible observations. Any observed differences require event-prevalence and state-distribution interpretation.
- Next measurement: inspect the Gate A2 GitHub result JSON, compare seen/unseen support and prediction quality, and then choose whether a truly diverse collection policy, a belief-state baseline, or revised held-out architecture comparison is the justified next experiment.

### Gate A2 measured result — GitHub Actions 39545943427 (commit d0b9602)

- Reproducibility: CI 39545943338 and GitHub diagnostics 39545943427 succeeded after a focused lint fix. Artifact \`bagi-e6-e9-e10-gate-a2\`, \`reachable_state_holdout/summary.json\`, contains the actual evaluation metrics.
- Evaluation design: 78 reachable **nonterminal** hidden states, exactly four actions each (312 state/action transitions, 3,224 simulator replay steps). The evaluator needed hidden flags to *enumerate* states but the existing checkpoint only received visible 5x5 observation and action. The original training split contained 191 distinct observation/action keys; the reachable graph has 224 unique observable-input/action keys, of which 33 were unseen in training.
- One-step predictive performance on seen versus unseen inputs: 279 vs 33 reachable transitions; cell accuracy **0.8575 vs 0.6109**, whole-view accuracy **0.2903 vs 0.0000**, reward MAE **0.0452 vs 0.1529**. Thus strong aggregate cell accuracy on familiar states hides much weaker complete next-observation predictions on novel inputs in this fixed Workshop world.
- Event caveat: the seen group had 11 positive event labels (3 switch, 2 key, 4 lock, 2 goal) and all 11 classified as positive at threshold 0.5; the unseen group had only 1 positive event (key pickup), which was missed. Unseen positive-event Brier was 0.996 but cannot be generalized from a single positive label.
- Interpretation: the model exhibits a large **association** between training-observation/action coverage and next-observation error on reachable states. Distribution composition and the single deterministic layout confound simple causal attribution; this does not establish that unseen inputs alone caused all failure or that a larger model fixes it.
- Next Gate A experiment: increase the number of distinct *nonprivileged* state/action histories under a matched environment-interaction budget, then evaluate on frozen reachable-graph partitions and a held-out policy/possibly new layout. Before retraining, document the amount of new coverage introduced. Alternatively, test a compact history/memory baseline once diverse experience and validation are available.

## 2026-10-21 — Gate A3: matched-budget data collection ablation

- Basis: count-based exploration is an established method for encouraging coverage of rarely visited observable state-action pairs. BAGI's next model should not be retrained until we know which collection approach provides genuinely distinct examples rather than repeated trajectory rows.
- Prior evidence: Gate A reported 201 distinct supervised transitions from 5,765 steps with 49/50 guided episodes exactly duplicated; Gate A2 found 85.7% vs 61.1% cell accuracy on seen vs unseen reachable observation/action inputs (whole-view accuracy 29% vs 0%).
- Hypothesis: replacing 45 of 50 redundant fixed guided episodes with additional real interaction, optionally selecting less-visited visible observation/action pairs, yields more independent coverage at the EXACT same original transition budget. A negative finding means the naive novelty policy has not helped, not that diverse data or memory is unnecessary.
- Controls: reuse original cached 5,765-step 50-guided/50-random dataset; for two alternative arms collect exactly 5,765 simulator transitions each, with five guided episodes followed by either uniform-random exploration or an epsilon=0.15 observable visitation-count exploration policy. Exploration seed 62031; original retained as frozen comparison. Record unique observation/action keys, distinct fully supervised transitions, reachable-key coverage relative to Gate A2 enumerated graph, novel keys versus original, and event-positive rows plus distinct event-positive keys.
- Restrictions: no model retraining, no hidden state accessed by controllers; simulator replay only for post hoc graph coverage. Same fixed Workshop layout, one exploratory seed, no transfer claim. Additional collection interactions used for diagnosis are separately reported (two arms x original budget).
- Status: proposed to CI and workflow; collect actual measurements before recommending any collector as a training-data improvement. Next: if either collector expands event-rich coverage, repeat with independent RNG seeds then train matched-budget model checkpoints; otherwise investigate history-aware/structured exploration and explicit memory under fair information constraints.

### Gate A3 measured result — GitHub Actions 39548197678

- CI run 39548197583 and experiment run 39548197678 passed after import-order fix at commit 872b679b9e65eeb9f87d8e384e4ee4515317eea1. Results artifact: \`bagi-e6-e9-e10-gate-a3\`, \`coverage_collection/summary.json\`.
- Every arm used exactly 5,765 real simulator transitions. Existing 50-guided/50-random training data covered 198/224 reachable visible-observation/action inputs (88.39%), 201 distinct supervised transitions. Replacing 45 repeated guided episodes with a five-guided plus ordinary random policy covered only 179/224 inputs (79.91%), 182 supervised transitions, with 15 observation/action inputs absent from the original. Five-guided plus count-based novelty covered 182/224 (81.25%), 185 supervised transitions, with 19 genuinely new input/action pairs absent from the original.
- Rare causal events were not preserved by reducing guided demonstrations: goal event-positive rows fell from 51 originally to 5 in each new arm; lock positive rows fell from 81 to 36 random and 7 novelty. These are correlated transition rows, not independent successes, and show that raw unique keys alone is insufficient.
- Interpretation: the naive novelty collector provides *complementary* state/action inputs (19 absent from the baseline) but worse *standalone* coverage and substantially less key/lock/goal support. Do not replace the baseline collector with it on this evidence. The comparison conflated the number of guided episodes and exploration policy; A4 isolates the exploration policy while retaining the same demonstration prefix.
- All findings are on one fixed layout, one exploratory RNG seed, and prior cached demonstrations. No improved learning, planning or generalization has been measured.

## 2026-10-21 — Gate A4: matched demonstration prefix, random vs count-based novelty

- Hypothesis: with the **same 50 guided demonstration episodes** in both arms, the visible-state/action visitation-count policy can improve distinct input-action coverage relative to random exploration without sacrificing the automatically reproduced rare positive events.
- Control: keep all original A3 five-guided arms for comparison; add two 50-guided arms, one random and one novelty, each with the exact 5,765 simulator-transition budget and exploration seed 62031, epsilon=0.15 for novelty. Each 50-guided arm necessarily has the same 800-step demonstration prefix, so differences are due to remaining exploration choices and RNG paths, not fewer demonstration episodes. The original legacy collection remains an additional contextual control.
- Measurements: unique inputs, fully supervised transitions, reachability coverage (224 observable-input/action keys on fixed layout), new keys versus original data, causal event-positive rows, unique event-positive input keys, and policy-specific paired deltas under the same demonstration count.
- Negative outcome rule: if novelty does not improve distinct coverage over matched random, discard it as the default data policy; prioritize information-matched memory/causal-history approaches or targeted coverage under a frozen held-out protocol. No learned model retraining in this cycle. Four prospective arms x 5,765 transitions = 23,060 simulator interactions; count separately from training data.
- Status: proposed to CI and the existing Gate A3 workflow; no A4 result claimed before it completes.

### Gate A4 measured result and decision — GitHub Actions 39548972850

- CI 39548972855 and GitHub research workflow 39548972850 succeeded at commit 56cefa0b21c9d919cd30a40e1ec0a4f7e78dc716. Artifact \`bagi-e6-e9-e10-gate-a3\`, \`coverage_collection/summary.json\`, includes five collection arms and the matched-policy deltas.
- Every arm had exactly **5,765 collected environment transitions**. The existing 50-guided/50-random historical dataset covered 198/224 reachable observation/action keys (88.39%) with 201 distinct supervised transitions.
- Matched 50-guided arms, exploration RNG seed 62031: **random reached 178 distinct observation/action keys** (79.46%) and 181 distinct supervised transition keys, with 15 keys absent from the historical dataset; **count-novelty reached only 89 keys** (39.73%) and 91 supervised keys, with **zero keys absent from the historical dataset**. Paired difference was -89 distinct input/action keys (-39.73 percentage points coverage). Both retained 50 guided goal events; random recorded 76 lock-opening rows against novelty's 50. These are within fixed-layout, same-guided-prefix, same-budget comparisons; distinct RNG paths differ after the exploratory actions begin.
- Matched 5-guided arms repeated the previous qualitative comparison: random 179 keys (79.91%), novelty 182 (81.25%), but novelty had 7 versus random 36 lock-opening rows. Both had only 5 goal-positive rows, compared with 50 in 50-guided arms.
- **Decision:** reject this globally accumulated visible-(observation,action)-count novelty selector as a default collection policy. It did not provide a reliable coverage improvement and could drastically reduce reachable-state visitation after a repeated expert prefix. We have NOT isolated the exact mechanism or shown that all exploration bonuses fail. The original training dataset remains unchanged and no improved trained-model performance is claimed.
- Research checkpoint: Gate A evidence now rejects two naive assumptions: (1) extra repeated guided trajectories are independent data; (2) replacing them with simple local novelty necessarily produces better experience. The goal is to establish a varied reachable starting-state/layout family and a leakage-resistant held-out test while preserving causal-event support; then compare matched-budget collection and a small information-matched GRU/history baseline. Avoid unprincipled uncertainty-weight or novelty-bonus tuning.
- Resource accounting: A3 new collection used 11,530 evaluation-only simulator interactions, and A4 used 23,060 across four prospective arms. Those interactions were diagnostic collection, **not** added to the existing model's 5,765-step training data, so no training sample-efficiency gain is demonstrated.

## 2026-10-21 — Gate B1: information-matched observation-history ambiguity ceiling

- Established principle: a current observation in a POMDP need not identify predictive state; observation/action history can maintain information about past causal events. This audit tests **whether such information helps in BAGI**, not whether a trained memory network is already better.
- Prior evidence: Gate A found 99.3% exact train/validation duplicate overlap; Gate A2 observed a whole-view accuracy collapse from 29% on familiar visible state/action keys to 0% on 33 previously unseen pairs; Gate A3/A4 showed unstructured count-based novelty did not improve event-rich experience under matched budgets.
- New hypothesis: among the 78 reachable Workshop v0.2 nonterminal states, one-step outcomes that are ambiguous from only the visible local observation/action become less ambiguous when up to 16 previously executed visible observation/action pairs are available.
- Design: enumerate one BFS-shortest real observation/action history for every reachable nonterminal hidden state, then enumerate four actions per state. Full hidden state is used *only to deduplicate reachable states*. Compare empirical majority-label ambiguity counts (next view+reward+causal events and reward+events alone) at memory windows [0,1,2,4,8,16]. Report real diagnostic simulator replay steps separately. Each memory input includes only visible observation/action history, current visible observation and the candidate action. This is not a learned predictor and does not retrain or use the old checkpoint.
- Controls/caveats: identical state-action targets at all memory windows; no distinct input is generated by accessing hidden state. The empirical majority-outcome ceiling is **in-sample**, not held-out accuracy; longer histories may simply create unique keys. Only a fixed layout and shortest-path histories are represented.
- Failure interpretation: if longer observed histories do not remove ambiguity, memory alone may not identify the crucial hidden variables under this data distribution and diverse history collection/active information gathering may be needed. If ambiguity decreases, next use a small learned GRU or deterministic belief-tracker under held-out trajectories and matched budgets, never compare in-sample ceilings as if they were learned-model accuracy.
- Status: submitted for separate lightweight Gate B1 workflow and CI; no Gate B1 measurement is claimed yet.

### Gate B1 measured result — GitHub Actions 39557134581

- Independent lightweight workflow completed successfully after the import-path repair (commit 6daba83); the same result was reproduced at the corrected lint commit f7a1c50. Four tests passed. A total of 78 reachable nonterminal states gave 312 observable state/action transitions, one BFS-shortest executed history per state.
- In-sample best-majority outcomes: zero history => **3 conflicting groups / 3 unavoidable errors** for complete next-view/reward/events, and 1 minimum event-only error. With one prior visible observation/action pair, all three conflicts resolved and minimum whole-outcome and event-only errors fell to **zero**. Larger windows 2,4,8,16 did not further change this shortest-path sample.
- This demonstrates that the selected observations can alias distinct outcomes and that short observed history contains useful disambiguating information. It is **not** neural prediction accuracy, held-out accuracy, transferable intelligence, or evidence that one-step memory always suffices on other action histories.
- Decision: advance to B2: separately trained/held-out *non-neural tabular lookup* on independent random action continuations from varied guided causal prefixes, comparing windows 0,1,2,4,8. Report key coverage/abstention beside conditional prediction accuracy so longer-history uniqueness cannot masquerade as predictive competence. Only then consider training a compact GRU and a genuinely held-out task layout.
## 2026-10-21 — Gate B2: held-out observation-history lookup across varied action paths

- Scientific question: does one or a few previous observations/actions improve *held-out* next-outcome prediction relative to the reactive view/action only baseline, without sacrificing too much key coverage?
- Design: retain the deterministic Workshop and a fixed scripted guided prefix to eight different causal stages (0,2,...,14) as a reproducible way to sample distinct hidden states. Collect 20 training and 10 held-out random-continuation episodes per prefix using independently seeded action RNG; continue for up to 25 real steps. Only continuation steps become labeled examples; prefixes are observed and counted as simulator interactions. Reset memory at episode boundaries and keep no privileged hidden flags. Frozen history windows 0,1,2,4,8 for both groups, with the exact same held-out transitions.
- Control: a transparent per-visible-input majority-outcome lookup, not a neural world model; report held-out exact-next-outcome accuracy conditional on observed keys, percentage of evaluation keys seen before, and all-evaluation accuracy counting unknown-key abstentions as incorrect. Also report empirical in-sample collision/ambiguity counts. This fixes B1's self-evaluation bias without claiming the environment contains new layouts.
- Falsification: if memory provides no better held-out correctness at a tolerable key coverage, stop pursuing pure history-key lookup; a compact generalizing recurrent state learner or action-history belief model is required. If memory improves conditional accuracy but mostly abstains, report that failure to generalize as a separate bottleneck, not as a memory success claim.
- Status: implementation submitted for lightweight workflow and CI; no B2 experimental result claimed yet.

### Gate B2 measured result — GitHub Actions 39557933041

- The lightweight scientific workflow completed successfully at commit 34b04f8 (and was repeated after a cosmetic test-fixture style fix); all seven targeted memory tests passed. A matched dataset of 240 episodes (160 training, 80 held-out, identical fixed Workshop) generated 5,621 continuation transitions and 7,301 total real simulator steps including 1,680 scripted prefix steps. No trained world-model checkpoint was updated.
- On 1,835 held-out continuation transitions, exact majority-outcome lookup accuracy *with unfamiliar input keys counted as incorrect* was 99.13% for current view/action only, 95.26% for one-step raw history, 72.26% for two-step, 18.20% for four-step, and 9.43% for eight-step. The fraction of held-out keys seen in training was 99.29%, 95.31%, 72.43%, 18.20%, and 9.43% respectively. Conditional accuracies for recognized keys remained high (>99%), but hide the drastic deterioration in coverage.
- An in-sample ambiguity metric decreased with increasing history length, but is not a generalization metric; key explosion makes whole-history memorization an unsuitable baseline. A reactive lookup achieves high accuracy on this heavily repeated fixed-layout trajectory distribution, but this does **not** validate a learned planner on unseen layouts or sparse causal events.
- Scientific decision: test a **compact causal belief summary** as an explicit hand-built reference, keeping the same training and held-out trajectories. This isolates whether remembering task-relevant information, rather than memorizing raw histories, can preserve test coverage. Any rule-based tracker must be labeled handcrafted and cannot be mistaken for self-learned intelligence.

## 2026-10-21 — Gate B3: compact observable causal belief vs raw-history lookup

- Established principle: a compact belief-state summary can preserve useful past information without exponential explosion in exact history keys. For Workshop, prior visible actions toward the SWITCH or KEY tiles can produce two Boolean event-history bits, without reading simulator position, key/door flags, or event labels at inference time.
- Design: replay the exact B2 seeded 240-episode dataset, but retain full observable action history (<= 100 steps) so the two bits can persist. Compare four non-neural lookup baselines on identical 1,835 held-out continuations: current view only, one raw previous step, eight previous raw steps, and a handcrafted two-bit observed-switch/observed-key memory plus the current view/action. Also measure the subset of held-out states whose current-observation/action key had conflicting training outcomes, if represented. Report unseen-key coverage and unknown-as-error accuracy. Training and evaluation real simulator interactions are counted separately from the frozen world-model training data.
- Falsification: if the handcrafted 2-bit reference does not improve held-out coverage/accuracy over current-view-only lookup on meaningful ambiguous examples, treat missing data diversity/representational learning as the next priority rather than claiming memory solves BAGI. If it helps, a small GRU may be justified as a *learnable approximation*, evaluated on episode-separated and eventually held-out layouts.
- Caveat: the tracker is rule-based with Workshop-specific prior knowledge; it is an upper-bound/reference mechanism, not general intelligence or transfer.
- Status: code submitted to GitHub memory-foundations CI, awaiting actual B3 measurement.

### Gate B3 measured result — GitHub Actions 39558640003

- CI 39558640027 and lightweight history experiment 39558640003 succeeded at commit d9ede6f0b3bd88706b7279f5c32a02e21207a30b; 11 targeted memory tests passed. Artifact \`bagi-gate-b1-b2-b3-memory\` contains JSON measurements for B1, B2 and B3.
- 240 fixed-layout executed episodes (160 train, 80 held-out), 1,835 held-out continuations: reactive lookup recognized 1,822 (99.29%) and got 1,819/1,835 outcomes correct when unknowns count as errors (99.13%); eight-step raw-history lookup recognized and correctly predicted only 173/1,835 (9.43%); a rule-based two-bit observed-switch/observed-key memory recognized 1,822 and correctly predicted all 1,822 (99.29%).
- In the 41 held-out transitions whose reactive visible-observation/action key had conflicting *training* outcomes, reactive predicted 38/41 correctly (92.68%) while the two-bit belief reference predicted 41/41 (100%). None of these 41 carried a positive event label. This is a very small, selected fixed-layout subset: no statistical generalization claim, no evidence of improved goal-reaching.
- **Decision:** compact causal information, not raw-history memorization, is the useful target for learning. But the existing environment has no start-position variation and the reactive baseline already scores high on its repeated layout. Before interpreting a GRU result as progress, introduce reproducible start-state diversity and later held-out layouts, then train the smallest recurrent model possible under matched data and compute. The two-bit expert is not learned intelligence.

## 2026-10-21 — Gate A5: seeded initial-state diversity under matched collection steps

- Motivating issue: Workshop.reset currently always starts at (1,1) irrespective of seed; old training and validation can therefore repeat nearly identical data, confounding memory and generalization experiments.
- Hypothesis: randomly sampling a legal starting cell within the pre-barrier chamber at reset, with RNG deterministically controlled by the Gym seed, improves visible observation/action coverage at equal real interaction budget while preserving scripted positive causal-event demonstrations.
- Intervention: backward-compatible start_mode='seeded_start_chamber' (default 'fixed' unchanged), eligible rows 1/2 and interior columns excluding the switch tile. This is **initial-state variation only, not new layout or task variation**. A scripted privileged expert uses reset position only to route to the historical start and then executes the same known goal-reaching guided sequence. Random exploration never receives hidden state; the world-model agent is not used. Fixed and randomized modes each collect exactly 5,765 real transitions with 50 scripted goal demonstrations, paired at seeds 512, 1024, 1536. Metrics include unique visible input/action keys, unique supervised transitions, event positive rows, distinct starts and separately counted expert-navigation steps.
- Limitations: expert routes have different lengths between arms, though total interactions and goal demonstrations match; three seeds and fixed geometry cannot validate transfer. No model is retrained, and data collected is diagnostic only. If diversity does not improve, reject this strategy instead of promoting it.
- Status: implementation submitted to lightweight workflow and CI; no A5 results claimed yet.

### Gate A5 measured result — GitHub Actions 39559482232

- The standalone seeded-start workflow 39559482232 succeeded with six targeted tests and a complete three-paired-seed collection; the next CI run fixes a test import-formatting issue. The historical fixed-start Workshop is unchanged by default.
- Exactly 5,765 collected real transitions per arm, 50 expert goal-completing demonstrations in every arm. Distinct visible observation/action keys for fixed versus seeded-varied starts were **204 vs 166** (seed 512), **212 vs 210** (1024), and **202 vs 202** (1536). Varied starts visited 13 legal pre-barrier cells per seed but did **not** improve overall input/action coverage. This includes unequal expert navigation lengths and identical world geometry.
- Decision: an optional seed-varying start provides useful controlled initial-state variation for future evaluation, but **is not** an improved data-collection policy on these results. Merely perturbing start position does not solve BAGI's coverage or hidden-state memory problem.

## 2026-10-21 — Gate A6: zero-shot symmetry transfer on mirrored causal Workshop

- Established baseline: a learned model should be evaluated on structurally related but nonidentical observations and action consequences under a fixed causal rule system, rather than new seeds on the exact same map.
- Intervention: add optional \`layout_mode="mirror_x"\` that horizontally reflects all positions, barriers and objects as a geometry symmetry while preserving the switch-door-key-lock-goal chain; default original Workshop behavior and previously trained checkpoints remain untouched. Mirror maps left/right actions and starts to their reflections. Tests verify expert path event-by-event equivalence and mirrored reachable-state graph size.
- Hypothesis: a world model trained only on the original layout may suffer measurable one-step predictive degradation on the reflected layout even though the causal rules are identical. That would expose orientation/generalization gaps; no degradation would be a useful positive transfer observation within this limited symmetry.
- Experimental design: restore the existing 50-guided frozen world-model checkpoint and original training transition logs; enumerate exact 78 nonterminal states x four actions separately for default and mirrored Workshop. Feed ONLY visible local observation and action to the trained model. Measure cell, whole-view, reward and causal-event prediction metrics, including keys seen/unseen in original training split and novel visible keys relative to original reachable graph. Identical model, no further real training, and 312 evaluation simulator transitions per layout (plus replay steps counted).
- Restrictions: mirrored layout has unchanged causal rule structure, so this cannot establish general AGI transfer or unseen causal mechanisms. Source world-state flags only construct evaluation state coverage, not model inputs. No model retraining or planner success claimed.
- Status: submitted to CI and E6 workflow; await mirrored-layout prediction artifacts before choosing model architecture.

### Gate A6 measured result — GitHub Actions 39560515033

- CI 39560515160 and the transfer workflow 39560515033 passed at commit 80b9affa224afba82092a0cc9043690567660923. Artifact: `bagi-e6-e9-e10-layout-transfer`, `layout_transfer/summary.json`.
- Frozen 50-guided world model, exhaustive 312 state/action evaluations per layout: default cell accuracy 0.8314 and whole-view accuracy 0.2596; reflected layout cell accuracy 0.7177 and whole-view accuracy 0.01282. Mirror has 220/224 visible action keys absent from the original training set, so the model is predominantly out of its original input distribution. The model only detected 1/3 mirrored switch, 1/3 mirrored key and 0/2 mirrored goal positives, whereas the default correctly detected 3/3, 2/3 and 2/2 respectively. Positive labels are rare and not independent.
- Interpretation: a substantial zero-shot orientation shift damages learned one-step view and causal-event predictions despite isomorphic rules. This is not evidence that higher model capacity would necessarily help.
- Next decisive test (A7): canonicalize the known exact horizontal reflection at the model interface, without retraining. Predict mirrored next observations after transforming into default orientation and flipping outputs back. If accuracy is restored, orientation equivariance is the mechanism; if not, investigate unmatched observation/action representations and geometry. Any success is a human-specified symmetry adapter, not learned transfer or AGI.

## 2026-10-21 — Gate A7: exact symmetry as an information-matched control

- Hypothesis: deterministic reflection of visible observations/actions into the trained model's original frame restores the frozen model's one-step prediction scores on mirrored Workshop, and exactly matches the original-layout reference in the ideal isomorphic case.
- Change: add a stateless zero-training canonicalization wrapper swapping right/left action IDs and flipping observation columns before world-model inference; flip only the predicted next-observation spatial axis back, not causal event or scalar reward predictions. Compare raw mirror, adapted mirror and original reference on exhaustive replayed reachable transitions under identical checkpoint; count all evaluator simulator steps separately. Include unit tests for action involution and transformed observation/logit correspondence.
- Restrictions: fixed rule symmetry known ahead of time, no hidden-state inputs into model, no trained parameters changed, no policy success claim. This evaluates a reusable equivariant inference transformation rather than new learned intelligence.
- Status: submitted for CI and evaluation; do not claim positive result until artifact confirms.

### Gate A7 verified measurement — GitHub Actions 39561554008

- CI 39561553908 and scientific workflow 39561554008 succeeded at commit 3b6cdb2ef36788ffed7500061b993fb9b897c102. Combined research artifact includes `symmetry_canonicalization/summary.json`.
- Exhaustive 312 reachable state/action evaluations per layout; same original frozen learned checkpoint and no new training. Mirrored raw cell prediction accuracy 0.717692 and whole-view accuracy 0.012821; canonicalized mirror cell accuracy 0.831410 and whole-view accuracy 0.259615. Reward MAE improved from 0.119458 to 0.056557; positive-event Brier from 0.477333 to 0.093218.
- Adapted mirror matched default-frame reference exactly on cell, whole-view, reward MAE and event Brier (zero reported metric differences). This confirms the supplied reflection transform and output/action alignment recover the known isomorphic orientation at inference time.
- Interpretation: orientation-specific failure is addressable with an exact **human-specified** symmetry, without learning a new world model. This is not evidence of learned equivariance, general transfer, better planning, or reaching the Workshop goal.
- Next decisive checkpoint: test equivalent closed-loop planning under reflection with matched random candidate sequences, model input/output transforms and action inversion. Keep the baseline planner's poor native-frame performance visible; report causal-stage progress and goal rates, not only one-step accuracy.

## 2026-10-21 — Gate A8: closed-loop symmetry-transfer planning

- Prerequisite: A7 conclusively recovered the frozen model's default one-step metrics on the exact mirrored map with a human-supplied orientation transform, no retraining. A7 did not measure planning.
- Question: does that same transform recover full-sequence switch/door/key/lock/goal performance, or does the original planner remain limited by its native weak imagination?
- Design: compare the original random-shooting MPC on default layout, unadapted mirror, and canonicalized mirror using the identical frozen checkpoint, three planner seeds [171,272,373], two episodes each, horizon eight, 128 candidates, and fixed observation/action-only world-model inputs. Each arm has an identical evaluation step cap and fixed starting state in its layout. Use actual environment event outcomes to score causal stages. Count evaluation simulator interactions separately from 128x8 imagined actions for every real decision; no model training. The known reflection wrapper is hand-coded, not learned. Candidate sequences are not coupled under reflection despite matching seeds, and this limits paired interpretation.
- Null outcome: if mirrored planning still fails to reach the goal, the A7 one-step transfer fix alone is insufficient. Do not attribute remaining failure solely to memory without a targeted test.
- Status: submitted to CI and research workflow; no behavioral improvement claimed before measurements complete.

### Gate A8 measured closed-loop result — GitHub Actions 39562785865

- CI 39562785846 and experiment workflow 39562785865 succeeded at commit d8bee706de00637b7467bcfa019e495d13ad7c9c after a one-line lint fix. A8 measurements are in artifact `bagi-e6-e9-e10-layout-transfer`, `mirror_planner_transfer/summary.json`.
- Three preselected planner seeds [171,272,373], two episodes each, horizon eight, 128 candidates, 100 real steps per episode, frozen original model checkpoint. Every condition used 600 evaluation simulator actions and 614,400 imagined one-step predictions. No new world-model training.
- Original default planner: switch 4/6, door/key/lock/goal 0/6. Unadapted mirrored planner: switch 0/6, door/key/lock/goal 0/6. Symmetry-canonicalized mirrored planner: switch **5/6**, door/key/lock/goal **0/6**.
- Interpretation: human-supplied coordinate symmetry restores known one-step predictions (A7) and improves early-stage switch activation in the mirror relative to an unadapted planner, but does not solve the longer causal chain. This small fixed-layout result is not evidence of reliable learned transfer, especially because the random candidate sequences are not reflection-paired across conditions. The original model still makes poor whole-observation predictions and long open-loop rollouts.
- Scientific checkpoint: treat orientation canonicalization as a validated narrowly scoped engineering adapter; prioritize an information-matched recurrent/belief model and/or stage-level planning on genuinely varied experience. Do not keep sweeping reflection or uncertainty weights. Next diagnostic should separate representation/memory from search by evaluating known scripted causal subgoals and exact observed event-history controls without privileged hidden-state input to learned policies.

## 2026-10-21 — REUSE-1: adopt maintained RL algorithms and standard partial-observability benchmarks

- **Established work checked** (official upstream source/documents verified): Stable-Baselines3 and SB3-Contrib 2.9.0 include PPO and RecurrentPPO (LSTM), permissively MIT licensed, current documented Gymnasium-compatible Box/discrete action support; API requires carrying LSTM state and resetting at episode start: https://sb3-contrib.readthedocs.io/en/master/modules/ppo_recurrent.html and https://pypi.org/project/sb3-contrib/. Farama MiniGrid 3.2.0 provides randomized standard Memory and DoorKey tasks plus ImgObsWrapper; https://minigrid.farama.org/ and https://github.com/Farama-Foundation/Minigrid/blob/main/minigrid/envs/memory.py. CleanRL has a published PPO-LSTM implementation, but its Atari-specific recipe requires more task-specific modification than the supported SB3-Contrib interface: https://docs.cleanrl.dev/rl-algorithms/ppo/.
- **Reuse decision:** use installed MiniGrid and its existing image-only observation wrapper and import upstream PPO and RecurrentPPO directly as an OPTIONAL dependency pair \`[rl]\` pinned to 2.9.0. No original policy networks, optimizer, PPO/LSTM implementation, map or reward are written. The only new code is thin Gymnasium integration, explicit LSTM episode reset and results accounting.
- **What existing research does NOT establish for BAGI:** task transfer/generalization and sample efficiency on standardized partially observable causal puzzles at fixed cost relative to BAGI's own model-based planner. The existing empirical Workshop results are one fixed layout and cannot establish these.
- **Contribution type:** third-party benchmark/method integration and compatibility reproduction, not a scientific invention.
- **Smoke-test design:** independently seeded Farama MemoryS13Random and DoorKey-6x6 tasks; train PPO and RecurrentPPO for EXACTLY 512 real simulator steps each, batch/rollout 32/64, one epoch per rollout, one fixed seed 17, evaluate separately on 3 different seeded episodes per algorithm. Save actual training and evaluation steps, goal successes/rewards, wall time, exact installed package versions and caveats in a separate 18-minute non-scheduled CI workflow. The learner sees only local image observations; scripts do not provide full state, mission text, expert actions or shaped rewards. This pilot is deliberately too small for sample-efficiency comparisons.
- **Falsification/stop rule:** if external libraries fail integration checks, fix only API/data conversions; if sparse-reward models do not solve tasks in 512 steps, do NOT present that as memory failure. Use measured wall-time and reproducibility as the go/no-go before authorizing larger multi-seed training. If standard benchmarks integrate, prioritize reproducible cross-layout/task transfer and memory behavior rather than adding more custom Workshop variants.
- **Status:** implementation submitted; no outcome or library-training success claimed before successful GitHub artifact.

## 2026-10-21 — REUSE-4: adopt established compositional task distribution before new learning code

- **Existing work checked:** NeurIPS 2024 XLand-MiniGrid (Nikulin et al.) and
  official Apache-2.0 package \`xminigrid==0.9.3\` with built-in
  \`RuleSet\`, \`AgentNearRule\`, \`AgentNearGoal\` and JAX environments. This
  supplies existing rule-changing task distributions and recurrent PPO
  baselines; no need to design another bespoke causal world or agent.
- **Security and resource decision:** the project's benchmark loader itself
  unpickles external downloaded bz2 tasksets; avoid it in credentialed
  automation. Do not download 60GB+ XLand learning histories or train a
  large baseline. Use only standard in-memory upstream primitives on a
  short CPU-limited smoke test with no downloaded task data.
- **Open question:** whether BAGI's eventual agents transfer across held-out
  *combinations of previously seen causal components* at fixed interaction
  budgets, not merely across a horizontal reflection of one geometry.
- **Experiment design:** choose two familiar source colors BLUE/YELLOW, two
  familiar result colors RED/GREEN. Train-labelled compositions are
  BLUE->RED and YELLOW->GREEN; held-out compositions BLUE->GREEN and
  YELLOW->RED. All use upstream \`AgentNearRule\` to transform a visible
  SQUARE tile into BALL on the established action and \`AgentNearGoal\` as
  goal. Verify SHA-256 disjoint encoded rulesets. Run only an untrained
  uniform-action evaluation baseline for 3 seeded episodes per task, capped
  at 40 real interactions each (480 maximum), counting actual steps.
  Training labels define future splits; NO learning is performed in REUSE-4.
- **Negative interpretation:** this only checks the borrowed benchmark
  interface and train/held-out task IDs. Zero random successes are expected
  and cannot be interpreted as failed learning or failure of transfer.
- **Status:** proposed to isolated CI and default CI. No learned capability,
  generalization improvement, or experiment success claimed yet.


### REUSE-4 verification and prerequisite refinement

- GitHub Actions 39576295564 passed the XLand 0.9.3 isolated suite at commit
  750fc466cbcb7bfbab138687586c72d1824ff3b7. Four rule-goal hashes
  were distinct, train vs held-out groups did not overlap, and all 12
  untrained random-policy evaluation episodes consumed 480 of 480 allowed
  simulator interactions, with 0/12 goals. This is environment wiring,
  NOT a learned transfer outcome.
- Source review identified a flaw in the *first task formulation*:
  \`AgentNearRule\` followed by \`AgentNearGoal\` may immediately satisfy
  the goal on the same activation action, not requiring a causal
  sequence. Correct the built-in goal primitive to \`AgentHoldGoal\`
  for the produced BALL tile. It checks pocket contents on pickup
  action, while the upstream \`AgentNearRule\` changes a neighboring
  SQUARE on a different action. Thus rule activation and object pickup
  are required separately, without new algorithms or controllers.
- Add a test pinning the upstream goal ID and rule ID so future changes
  cannot silently make the challenge a one-step trigger again.
- Status: refined version submitted for revalidation; no learned
  competence, end-to-end solvability or multi-layout transfer claimed.


## 2026-10-21 — REUSE-5: establish bounded solvability of existing XLand causal compositions

- **Existing work reused:** exact \`xminigrid==0.9.3\` XLand room, six-action simulator, \`AgentNearRule\`, \`AgentHoldGoal\` and classical, well-established shortest-path **breadth-first search**. No original neural architecture or RL method. The diagnostic BFS is tiny navigation glue over (position, heading), used as a privileged capability ceiling.
- **Scientific prerequisite:** REUSE-4 established four SHA256-distinct train/held-out source/result rule-goal pairs but a uniform-random agent achieved 0/12 within 40 steps (run 39576647873). That does not prove the new task design is solvable at the same interaction cap. Check this **before** expensive learning.
- **Method:** for each of the same 4 tasks x evaluation seeds [5100,5101,5102], read the simulator's exact initial grid/agent orientation solely for the diagnostic oracle. BFS to a traversable adjacent cell facing the source SQUARE; execute the resulting turn/forward actions in the *unmodified* environment, then action 0 to trigger \`AgentNearRule\`, then action 3 to pick up the produced BALL satisfying \`AgentHoldGoal\`. Report executed steps, BFS expansions, intermediate transformation, carried result and true simulator goal reward. Cap each trajectory at the same 40 real actions. Predeclared tests cover shortest heading-aware paths and unreachable objects. Same-task random result appears as **separately measured reference**, not an information-matched control.
- **Limits:** full-state object position and goal/result identity are privileged inputs to this diagnostic; never supply them to a learned/observation-only policy. BFS proves only navigational task feasibility for these specific seeded layouts, not intelligence, adaptation, compositional generalization, or an independent learner baseline.
- **Stop/negative rule:** if the privileged ceiling cannot solve the tasks, revise their specification or cap before training anything. If the ceiling solves them, retain its true minimal action counts and adopt existing upstream recurrent/meta-RL implementations for an actual training vs held-out recombination study; do not build RL anew.
- **Status:** pushed with bounded XLand workflow; no solvability result claimed until CI and artifact complete.

### REUSE-5 VERIFIED result and benchmark promotion gate — GitHub Actions 39578198961

- Experimental commit \`16cfc77f14a2d5ee34a581c65d977df78572faa4\` passed two full GitHub CI jobs \`39578199037\`, \`39578203103\` and dedicated XLand experiment \`39578198961\`; artifact \`bagi-xland-composition-contract\`, \`xland_composition_oracle/summary.json\`.
- **Hard simulator evidence:** privileged classical BFS route + upstream \`AgentNearRule\` forward action + \`AgentHoldGoal\` pickup solved all **12/12** task/seed episodes, including all six labelled held-out cases, within **7–11 real actions** per episode (108 total); object transformation and subsequent acquisition were both observed for every episode. Search expanded 1,468 position/heading states in aggregate. Prior same-seed untrained uniform-random policy had **0/12**, 480 real interactions, on the exact same task configuration (run \`39576647873\`). Privileged oracle has more information than the random agent, so these are a solvability bound and contextual floor, not a fair skill comparison.
- **Crucial benchmark deficiency discovered from actual oracle traces:** for each of seeds 5100, 5101 and 5102, the four *different* color-pair tasks yielded **identical action sequences**; per-seed lengths 7, 11 and 9 across all four rulesets. The ruleset SHA256 train/held-out split is syntactically disjoint but **behaviorally indistinguishable under this optimal-navigation strategy**. Thus a policy could solve all four by learning generic "find any square, trigger transformation, pick up" behavior, without transferring a novel causal composition. This weak task design is **NOT approved as evidence of compositional generalization**. Do not train a costly recurrent PPO just to report cross-color held-out success as novelty.
- **Established work revisited:** upstream XLand-MiniGrid \`training/train_meta_task.py\` already implements JAX multi-task recurrent PPO (adapted from PureJaxRL); its \`training/nn.py\` includes \`ActorCriticRNN\`, and \`configs/meta.yaml\` requests 10 billion simulator steps and 16,384 environments in the paper setup. That's not a sensible unrestricted GitHub Actions run. Its \`xminigrid.load_benchmark\` downloads remote task files then calls unsafe pickle deserialization; avoid that path in credentialed automation.
- **Next legitimate research gate:** reuse upstream XLand production-rule and goal diversity, not just source/result colors, to create a behaviorally discriminative *held-out* task split. Before training, require (1) distinct causal action requirements or multi-stage rules, (2) solvability and bounded real actions by a privileged oracle, (3) an explicit policy-information protocol (what rules, task identity, previous rewards/histories are observable), (4) zero leakage across held-out rule-goal structures, and (5) paired, fair established model-free/recurrent baselines at predeclared finite compute. Prefer a safe upstream standard benchmark conversion or verified in-memory generation over new benchmark algorithms.
- **Stop criterion:** REUSE-5 complete. Do not re-run the same color-recombination smoke test or treat oracle success as learned progress.


## 2026-10-21 — REUSE-6: structural compositional contrast using upstream rules

- **Established work**: official XLand-MiniGrid \`AgentHoldRule\` (tile in agent's pocket becomes a new tile on pickup), \`AgentNearRule\` (adjacent grid tile transforms on forward), \`AgentHoldGoal\`, \`RuleSet\`, and previously checked conventional shortest-path BFS for *diagnostic only*. No new environment dynamics, rewards, or RL algorithm.
- **Why**: REUSE-5 found color-recombination held-outs were behaviorally trivial: every rule solved with the same oracle trace. Need a reproducible task that requires a new action composition, not just novel colors.
- **Preregistered train-labelled component skills**: \`hold\`: pick up BLUE SQUARE to produce held RED BALL (one action after navigation); \`near\`: transform nearby RED BALL to GREEN BALL by forward then pick it up (two actions). **Held-out composition**: start with BLUE SQUARE, convert held BLUE SQUARE to RED BALL; put RED BALL back on the floor; use adjacent forward action to transform it to GREEN BALL; pick up GREEN BALL as goal. Recipe includes pickup, putdown, forward, pickup (four distinct suffix actions). All three tasks use only the already implemented XLand primitive rules and goal checks.
- **Crucial behavioral control**: run each *training* task's short action suffix against the *same held-out* initial state/seed. If either succeeds, this is not a sufficiently discriminative task. Run the chain recipe separately on the same state and require actual upstream simulator reward. Count all real simulator steps including controls and BFS expansions. Three reproducible seeds [5100, 5101, 5102] and 40-step episode cap.
- **Information caveat**: recipes and navigation oracle access privileged full grid and rules; they are benchmark construction/solvability diagnostics, not intelligent agents. A future learner will see only regular partial observations and previous interactions; task disclosure or rule inference must be specified before RL training. If 3/3 held-outs cannot be solved or primitive replays unexpectedly solve them, reject this specification and do not train.
- **Scientific outcome sought**: a genuinely behavior-distinguishing *existing-rule* compositional test with no new model code. A successful oracle is necessary but not sufficient for learned compositional generalization. Training effort is deferred until protocol passes.
- **Status:** submitted for CI and simulator verification, not yet a measured result.

We also established a rewarded put-down curriculum primitive to cover action 4. Behavioral cloning is a *known supervised-learning competence control*, not a replacement for long-term causal learning.

The teacher can use privileged state for *labels* only.

Verify *positive train-task skill acquisition* before making claims about composed generalization; otherwise stop and investigate task observability or DAgger using existing methods, not a new algorithm.
