## 2026-10-21 — REUSE-10: isolate class imbalance without collecting new expert data

- **Existing work reused:** PyTorch \`WeightedRandomSampler\` with inverse frequency per XLand native action class and existing \`imitation.BC\` learner. No new policy/optimizer/curriculum/world dynamics code; use exactly the same successful 256-label three-skill XLand dataset from REUSE-9 and the same two model seeds/held-out evaluation seeds.
- **Hypothesis:** failing to predict necessary pickup and put-down actions is driven in part by training-label imbalance; balancing sample exposures can increase action-3/4 recall on ORIGINAL unchanged expert examples and possibly closed-loop primitive success, without adding one environment interaction.
- **Design:** compare ordinary shuffled 256-label data versus 256-with-replacement sampled action-balanced indices using existing WeightedRandomSampler. Both use the same 12 epochs and BC architecture, hence **3,072** label exposures per model, same original distinct expert pool and zero added collection interactions; balanced batches may repeat rare rows and omit some originals, so report number of unique indices sampled. Evaluate train-label average accuracy plus **per-action recalls 0–5 on the unchanged dataset**, and actual 40-step held-out-seed train primitive and chain goals. Two preregistered seeds [17,29], three episode seeds [9100,9101,9102]. No tuning on held-out metrics.
- **Null case:** better pickup/put-down supervised recall but unchanged task success points to execution-distribution shift/ambiguous task identity; no recall improvement points to inadequate sample support or representational learning. A single success is descriptive only, not statistical evidence. Follow up using existing DAgger with a fair on-policy expert-query budget rather than inventing a new algorithm.
- **Status:** submitted to CI; no actual balanced outcome asserted until measured artifact.


### REUSE-10 verified measurement — class balancing improves action recall, NOT goal success

- **Execution:** GitHub run \`39590750677\` and CI runs \`39590750564\`/\`39590758064\` all passed at commit \`548d4ab50ab98adf7e88404dfca07e69dbd7670e\`; artifact \`bagi-existing-bc-balanced\`, ID \`12342161679\`, \`summary.json\`, inspected from downloaded JSON. Previous initial import-order/lint retries failed without training and do not count as results.
- **Controlled source data:** both \`imitation.BC\` conditions reused the exact same 256 XLand partial-observation/action labels from the three primitive skills (31 successful expert episodes, 260 oracle and 260 replay simulator steps, zero rejected seeds). Original action frequencies were {0:157 forward, 1:36 right, 2:23 left, 3:30 pickup, 4:10 put-down}. The held-out \`hold_then_near\` chain was never demonstrated. Training seeds 17/29, 12 epochs of 256 sampled examples each (**3,072 optimization sample exposures per policy**), evaluation seeds 9100–9102 on each of 4 tasks. Comparison changed ONLY ordinary shuffling versus upstream \`torch.utils.data.WeightedRandomSampler\` with inverse per-action class frequency, sampling with replacement. Balance selected 123 distinct row indices out of the 256-example pool in each seed (versus ordinary all 256); no new demonstrations were collected.
- **Supervised metrics on the ORIGINAL non-resampled 256 labels:** ordinary fit **65.625%** for both model seeds, with action-0 recall 100%, action-3 pickup recall **0%**, action-4 putdown recall **0%**. Class balancing increased pickup recall to **90%** in both seeds and put-down recall to **100%** (seed17) / **90%** (seed29). Overall unweighted fit fell to 32.03125% and 55.46875% respectively. The balanced models selected all classes 0–4 in the sampled labels; action 5 was absent from all positive demonstration labels.
- **Closed-loop behavioral metrics:** BOTH ordinary and balanced learners solved **0/3** episodes for hold, near, rewarded putdown and held-out two-rule chain at both policy seeds. Every learner consumed its 40-step episode cap (12 episodes x 40 = 480 eval simulator steps per policy). Balanced model17 actually chose pickups (action3) on hold/near/chain but never putdown (action4) on those tasks; model29 actually chose putdown (action4) but still failed goal completion. The ordinary learners chose only forward/right in every rollout.
- **Interpretation:** inverse-frequency sampling repairs the observed *rare-action recognition* failure of REUSE-9, but **does not repair closed-loop skill execution**, even on training tasks. This is a well-controlled negative; it does NOT show that composition or memory is impossible. Open explanations include behavioral cloning distribution shift, insufficient demonstration-state coverage, unobserved/ambiguous task identity or required history, and inadequate exposure/feature representation. Avoid arbitrary reweighting/hyperparameter sweeps, ever-increasing redundant demos or custom RL rewrites.
- **Next high-value diagnostic:** using the same three-skill expert data, measure exact visible-image+direction ambiguity (identical observations with incompatible expert actions), effective distinct expert-observation coverage, and whether a task-instruction variable is required for an information-matched learner. If no intrinsic aliasing explains the issue, use established HumanCompatibleAI DAgger on-policy correction rather than invent a new trainer, with strictly counted expert queries, simulator interactions, independent train/eval seeds, and no held-out-chain expert queries. Reuse upstream XLand task/reward dynamics; privilege may provide expert labels but never learned-policy features.
- **Checkpoint:** REUSE-10 is complete. Do not re-run ordinary or class-balanced BC on the same dataset/seed budgets; future work must answer a newly isolated causal/learning question.


## 2026-10-21 — REUSE-11 VERIFIED: observed-action aliasing in train-only XLand expert examples

- **Provenance:** research implementation commit `4c99a05e04d83a1d7bf375068bd4a43c38dea1d6`, successful dedicated GitHub Actions run `39597853860`, artifact `bagi-xland-observation-aliasing` ID `12344607429`, `summary.json` inspected. GitHub CI runs `39597853850` and `39597860600` succeeded. These are actual measured artifacts, not design predictions.
- **Existing work reused:** the unchanged training-only XLand-MiniGrid 0.9.3 3-skill oracle collector from REUSE-9/10, exact learner-visible image + direction observations, NumPy exact grouping/standard action counts. There is no policy training, custom learning method, or held-out-chain demonstration; this is diagnostic integration and empirical evaluation.
- **Collection contract and real simulator cost:** deterministic re-collection of precisely **256** labeled expert observations from **31** successful training-skill episodes (hold 11, near 10, putdown 10). **260 real expert-generator actions + 260 real replay actions = 520** simulator steps; zero rejected attempts, so uncounted rejected-seed steps were zero on this execution. Skill action counts exactly matched REUSE-10: {0:157, 1:36, 2:23, 3:30, 4:10}. Only original training seeds 5100–5110 were used; no new learner interactions or separate evaluation episodes.
- **Actual measured aliasing:** observation feature width **54**; **180 distinct exact visible image+direction vectors** across 256 decisions; **196 distinct (visible observation, action) pairs**; **41 groups repeated**, 139 singleton groups. **16** identical-observation groups required conflicting expert actions, covering **52 rows**. Even an oracle memorizing the majority label per exact visible vector can fit at most **234/256 = 91.40625%** of these labels (**22 unavoidable disagreements on this finite observed pool**). This is an empirical lookup ceiling on the SAME data, not a learning generalization ceiling.
- **Task identity diagnosis:** 5 conflict groups involved multiple training tasks, but **all 16 also contained within-task conflicting actions**. Conditioning exact lookup on oracle task identity did **not** change the empirical 234/256 ceiling; no group was completely disambiguated by just task ID. This weakens the explanation that mere missing task identity accounts for observed expert contradictions. It does NOT show hidden task information never matters in closed loop or that recurrence is sufficient.
- **Causal-action coverage:** pickup action 3 appeared in **30 labels at 22 distinct visible observations**; **10 pickup-labeled rows** shared visible observations with other expert actions. Put-down action 4 appeared in **10 labels at 9 distinct visible observations** and **0 put-down rows** were label-ambiguous on these exact samples. Rare-action policy failure cannot be ascribed purely to contradictory put-down labels in the source dataset.
- **Interpretation and stop rule:** exact within-task visible-state aliasing is now MEASURED, while distribution-shifted learner rollouts and short history utility remain unknown. Do NOT repeat ordinary-versus-balanced BC, infer that memory is learned, or train on the heldout `hold_then_near` chain. REUSE-11 answers only the narrow first-five diagnostic questions; it does not provide trained-policy off-expert occupancy traces.
- **Next falsifiable, reuse-first checkpoint (REUSE-12):** On the identical training-only 256-label collector and unchanged action distribution, test deterministic majority lookups using the current visible vector plus **0, 1 or 4 preceding expert actions** reset at episode ends; these are preregistered fixed windows, not a tuned architecture search. Report signature uniqueness, repeated groups, residual contradictions and lookup fit. Beware singleton inflation: improved exact fit alone is not proof an LSTM/GRU can learn the skill. If recent action history does not resolve substantial contradictions, investigate teacher ambiguity/partial visibility and existing DAgger off-expert correction with strictly measured learner interactions and oracle queries. If it does, next establish an information-matched existing recurrent learner before larger training.


### REUSE-12 verified measurement — four previous actions resolve most exact expert-label conflicts

- **Execution:** REUSE-12 implementation and REUSE-11 permanent log in commit `571a4f985b89b6cb8a105ab0411b378f0cd70b58`. The dedicated GitHub run `39598858760` finished successfully; its saved `bagi-xland-history-aliasing` artifact ID `12345351769` (`summary.json`) was downloaded and inspected. CI runs `39598858491` and `39598876579` completed successfully. The prior REUSE-9 BC workflow was automatically retriggered by REUSE-11's collector-provenance change, also completed, and is **not** counted as a new independent learning result.
- **Reuse and falsifiable uncertainty:** existing XLand 0.9.3 three-task dataset collector; NumPy exact observed-feature grouping and collections.Counter; no new learner, trainer, oracle task ID, hidden geometry feature, or held-out-chain demonstration. Question: would **1 or 4 immediately preceding expert actions** (available to the partially observed agent in principle, reset at each episode boundary) make previously contradictory labels distinguishable? Preregistered 0/1/4 context lengths are audit conditions, not learned-model hyperparameter tuning.
- **Exact dataset and costs:** 256 labeled decisions, original action counts {0:157,1:36,2:23,3:30,4:10}; 31 successful teacher episodes, 260 generator + 260 replay simulator steps = **520 real simulator interactions**, zero rejected collection seeds; **0 policy training interactions, 0 rollout evaluations**. The action and observation-only lookup baseline reproduced REUSE-11 exactly, making the comparison auditable.

| Observable feature input | Distinct exact signatures | Repeated groups | Conflicting groups | Conflicting rows | Empirical maximum correct /256 | Unavoidable errors |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Current local observation + direction only | 180 | 41 | 16 | 52 | 234/256 (91.40625%) | 22 |
| Same + previous 1 action | 202 | 38 | 8 | 23 | 245/256 (95.703125%) | 11 |
| Same + previous 4 actions | 228 | 28 | 2 | 4 | 254/256 (99.21875%) | 2 |

- **What actually changed:** using last four observed/executed expert actions as history reduces contradictions on the SAME expert examples by 20/22 errors relative to no history. This is a measured signal that recent action sequence contains relevant context for predicting the existing teacher. It does **not** establish that the teacher is uniquely correct, that agent memory generalizes off expert trajectories, or that an LSTM would learn these rules.
- **Critical caveat:** history signatures become more nearly unique (180 to 228), so a larger same-dataset majority-lookup ceiling can arise from memorization rather than transferable belief representation. There are still 28 repeated history-signature groups at window 4, of which 2 are action-conflicting. This is an information-boundary diagnostic, **not** a closed-loop skill acquisition result and not proof that task-identity ambiguity is irrelevant everywhere.
- **Next meaningful learned-skill experiment:** reuse HumanCompatibleAI `imitation.BC` with an information-matched four-previous-action input constructed from training-only trajectories and from the learner's own prior actions at evaluation. Compare against the previously measured ordinary memoryless class-balanced BC reference, with the identical 256 source labels, original action frequencies, held-out chain frozen, policy seeds [17,29] and evaluation seeds [9100,9101,9102], and no privileged runtime policy features. Pre-register closed-loop **training-primitive success as the primary gate**; report rare-action recall and matching real simulator interaction counts, not only supervised fit. Keep model architecture and optimization exposures as matched as practical. If this small established-method control remains 0/3 on all training tasks despite fitting expert rows, prefer a rigorously budgeted existing DAgger rollout-correction diagnostic over another sampling-weight or arbitrary model-size sweep. Do not escalate compute based only on empirical lookup accuracy.


### REUSE-13 preregistration — training an established memoryless BC with four prior actions

- **Existing work:** keep HumanCompatibleAI `imitation.algorithms.bc.BC` 1.0.1, XLand-MiniGrid 0.9.3, PyTorch's existing inverse-frequency `WeightedRandomSampler`, and the existing exact REUSE-10 training-only expert collection. A fixed four-action one-hot history is a simple input adaptation to a feedforward learned agent, NOT a new policy, recurrent architecture, imitation algorithm or proof of intelligence.
- **Unresolved scientific hypothesis:** REUSE-12 found an exact expert-label lookup increase 234/256 to 254/256 when four preceding expert actions were supplied; can a learned policy actually use a history derived from its OWN actions to execute individual skills in closed loop? Merely increasing lookup fit could reflect extra memorization.
- **Frozen controlled design:** exactly 256 training-only three-skill teacher labels and the same collection seeds and class-balanced sampler as REUSE-10, 12 imitation.BC training epochs, model seeds [17,29], evaluation seeds [9100,9101,9102] and 40-step episode caps. Keep the held-out `hold_then_near` oracle completely outside training. Use local XLand visible features plus **four preceding one-hot actions** (no oracle state/task ID); the learner's own previous actions, never teacher actions, populate history during evaluation. The original memoryless class-balanced REUSE-10 reference recorded 0/3 on each primitive/chain task for both seeds under these evaluation conditions.
- **Prespecified measurement and stop rule:** training-label accuracy and rare action recalls, raw source/sampled action counts, actual teacher-generation/replay interactions, CPU training seconds, and all closed-loop train-task/held-out goal successes and evaluation interactions. Also measure exact off-expert learner-input coverage per rollout against the original 256 expert inputs to distinguish supervision from on-policy visitation, while acknowledging unseen eval seeds can start outside that pool. **Primary gate is positive train-task goal success, NOT training accuracy or the held-out chain zero.** If no training skills succeed, do not escalate recurrent architectures or retune class balance on these tiny data; first consider an existing `imitation` DAgger on-policy correction with measured oracle queries and strict held-out segregation.
- **Status:** code and dedicated workflow prepared; no trained result asserted before successful GitHub artifact review.


### REUSE-13 VERIFIED — four-action-history Behavioral Cloning fails closed-loop skills

- **Provenance and release checks:** dedicated workflow `39599925595` finished success at source commit `59cdf772a193559459479ee78f52fee36fae4781`; artifact `bagi-existing-bc-action-history`, ID `12346305807`, `summary.json` downloaded and measured. Both CI runs `39599925468`/`39599932430` completed successfully. Passing CI is NOT a trained-policy success.
- **Method reused:** unchanged HumanCompatibleAI `imitation.BC` training algorithm, established PyTorch `WeightedRandomSampler` inverse-action class balancing, XLand 0.9.3 three-task training-only 256-label expert pool, original policy seeds 17/29 and evaluation seeds 9100–9102 with 40-action caps. Only added four previous one-hot **agent-own** actions to learner-visible local image and heading at closed-loop evaluation; expert previous actions were used solely to assemble TRAINING examples. No oracle coordinates, hidden rules, full grid, or heldout-chain teacher labels given to policy.
- **Real resource accounting:** 31 successful expert collection episodes (11 hold,10 near,10 putdown), **260 teacher-generator + 260 replay = 520 simulator steps**, 0 rejected seeds. Each trained model had **3,072 optimization sample exposures** across 12 BC epochs, **123 distinct resampled original expert rows**. CPU training seconds: seed17 **0.165**, seed29 **0.154** (does not include setup, JAX compiling, collection or evaluation). Training optimization uses no environment steps; all **24 evaluated episodes** (2 models x 4 tasks x 3 seeds) exhausted 40 steps each, for **960 evaluation simulator steps**. Total measured successful-demo + replay + evaluation steps = **1,480** (excluding any non-step JAX compilation).
- **Training examples:** unchanged action counts {0:157,1:36,2:23,3:30,4:10}. Class-balanced history BC on the unchanged original examples achieved **57.03125%** unweighted expert-label fit (policy seed17), **54.6875%** (seed29). Pickup recall **100%** / **86.6667%**; put-down recall **90%** / **90%**. These are supervised metrics, NOT acquired skill competence.

| Model seed | Hold | Near | Rewarded put-down | Held-out chain |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 17 | 0/3 | 0/3 | 0/3 | 0/3 |
| 29 | 0/3 | 0/3 | 0/3 | 0/3 |

- **Direct rollout evidence:** all **960/960** policy-visited exact observation-plus-four-own-action-history input rows were absent from the original 256-example expert training-input set; first unseen input at decision **0 in every one of the 24 evaluation episodes**. This strict equality test is descriptive: independent evaluation layouts/seeds can start outside the finite training input set, and it does not prove failure to generalize to nearby features or isolate error compounding. Seed17 overselected action 4 (100/120 put-down task actions), despite zero goal success. Seed29 emitted action 4 frequently but no pickup action 3 in any evaluated task. Both policies' actual closed-loop action profiles diverged from required causal sequences.
- **Conclusion:** REUSE-12's higher 254/256 history-conditioned expert lookup bound **did not transfer into learned closed-loop competence** using standard class-balanced offline BC at this dataset budget. The available data support inadequate expert-state coverage and/or off-policy learner distribution shift as active bottleneck candidates, not an inference that causality or memory methods are impossible. We cannot scientifically assess heldout causal transfer until the learner can complete training primitives.
- **Falsifiable next gate, not yet executed:** audit whether the existing privileged XLand training-skill teacher can reliably provide corrective labels from states VISITED BY a weak policy (not just initial-seed scripted paths), without ever labeling the held-out chain. If this oracle callback is valid, reuse HumanCompatibleAI `imitation`'s established DAgger rather than create a new imitation algorithm. Pre-register a small expert-query and real-interaction budget, independent heldout evaluation seeds, and the same ordinary memoryless/class-balanced baseline reference. Record trajectories, counts and null outcomes. Do not just repeat balancing sweeps, grow demonstration counts arbitrarily, claim dataset exact-match novelty equals failure of functional generalization, or start a resource-heavy recurrent trainer without a genuine competence gate.


## 2026-10-21 — REUSE-14 preregistration: is the training-only expert usable for off-policy labels?

- **Previously measured prerequisite:** REUSE-13 (artifact `12346305807`) found 0/3 closed-loop success on every individual skill for both seeds, while the fixed class-balanced history learner visited 960/960 exact state+history inputs absent from its 256 expert examples. Exact nonmembership is not itself a generalization test, but corrective expert feedback is the next established approach to investigate.
- **Reused published method:** DAgger's original online expert querying and dataset aggregation, as provided by HumanCompatibleAI `imitation.algorithms.dagger.SimpleDAggerTrainer` or `DAggerTrainer`: https://imitation.readthedocs.io/en/latest/algorithms/dagger.html . The existing XLand 0.9.3 rules/rewards and BAGI's already-reviewed privileged BFS helper are reused for diagnostic teacher labels. DAgger itself is not being reimplemented or run in REUSE-14; the missing BAGI-specific component is a trustworthy first-action expert callback from an *arbitrary, ongoing state* rather than a reset-only reference trajectory.
- **Falsifiable gate:** evaluate three training tasks, independent diagnostic seeds [7300,7301,7302], 40-step cap, and two fixed weak scripts plus an event-interrupted oracle prefix (where nonterminal). The teacher is allowed privileged CURRENT state only to generate a label. Replan one expert action from the actual off-path state, execute it in the simulator, and repeat until reward or cap. Test both usual empty-pocket navigation and near-rule-produced green ball/putdown pocket-square recovery. Count every roll-in, expert-generation, recovery simulator step and every teacher query. No held-out `hold_then_near` oracle states, goal labels, or learner features; report recovery success/failure by case even when negative.
- **Interpretation and stop:** successful recovery would make an established DAgger integration technically plausible, NOT prove policy learning. Failed recovery or repeated failure under some phase means the oracle callback must be repaired or the DAgger proposal deferred. Scripted weak scripts are not REUSE-13 trained policies, and intermediate expert prefixes are diagnostic only. Stop the experiment if on-policy teacher labels cannot be scientifically trusted.
- **Status:** implementation and dedicated GitHub experiment workflow submitted; no measured result inferred from source commit alone.


### REUSE-14 verified — off-path train-only expert correction is feasible

- **Actual evidence:** dedicated XLand CPU workflow `39603838885` passed at `4134e6f4f43dcd44724a1a1e1d61b22ee7a06fcd`, artifact `bagi-dagger-teacher-feasibility` ID `12347541556` with `summary.json` inspected. Initial `dde59d5d9c696762219ba9c54f41a33b83148d76` run `39603677008` stopped on two lint/import warnings BEFORE simulating any episodes; it is an engineering failure, not a negative experiment.
- **Question/approach:** reuse XLand's real reward and transition rules, BAGI's existing privileged BFS, and separate training-only causal task recipes to generate corrective first actions in CURRENT states, rather than re-running reset-only expert action trajectories. Teacher reads privileged map and inventory only to supply diagnostic ACTION LABELS. No learned policy trained, no heldout-chain state queried, no arbitrary new imitation algorithm.
- **Measured outcomes:** fixed training tasks hold, near and putdown; independent diagnostic seeds 7300, 7301, 7302; 40-step cap; two six-step weak scripts plus event-interrupted expert navigation where nonterminal. Corrective teacher solved **24/24 off-path continuations**: hold 6/6, near 9/9, putdown 9/9; includes near-task green-transformed state and putdown carried-square stage. **173** corrective label queries and **173** corrective simulator steps; **156** weak/perturbation roll-in simulator steps; **58** additional expert-generation simulator steps for post-event setup, for **387 total counted real simulator steps**. No validation was inferred from action labels alone; all success events were obtained from actual XLand reward.
- **Important limits:** weak scripts are NOT learned-policy rollouts, and the teacher has privileged simulator state so is NOT an information-matched competitor. All 24 recoveries are within one small fixed family; no general arbitrary-state oracle guarantee, policy skill acquisition, or compositional transfer is demonstrated. This is a positive *teacher callback feasibility* prerequisite only.
- **Next research decision:** HumanCompatibleAI `imitation` v1.0.1 includes `SimpleDAggerTrainer` and `LinearBetaSchedule` in upstream source (https://github.com/HumanCompatibleAI/imitation/blob/v1.0.1/src/imitation/algorithms/dagger.py). DAgger itself solves dataset aggregation; a thin XLand Gymnasium wrapper and a privileged teacher BasePolicy adapter are necessary because original XLand uses JAX functional environment states rather than an SB3 VecEnv. Build only this compatibility layer, not a new imitation algorithm. A small DAgger integration tests whether feedback from learner-visited states yields any positive primitive closed-loop reward.

### REUSE-15 VERIFIED — existing online DAgger executes, but learns no closed-loop primitive

- **Source and actual artifact:** implementation commit `a735acbd860f017baae8b0645b0e01a0eaede574`, lint/closure commit `f1bf72e7466336a185c7f293c663b07f8d519288`. Successful dedicated upstream DAgger workflow `39605165443`, artifact `bagi-existing-dagger`, ID `12346764156`, `summary.json` downloaded and inspected. The earlier workflow `39604831992` failed LINT before any training and is not a scientific null outcome. Both normal CI checks on corrected source should be inspected independently.
- **Established algorithms actually used:** HumanCompatibleAI `imitation.algorithms.dagger.SimpleDAggerTrainer` v1.0.1, upstream `LinearBetaSchedule(1)` and upstream `imitation.BC`; XLand-MiniGrid v0.9.3 simulator. BAGI supplied a small Gymnasium bridge for the original functional JAX environment and a current-privileged-state teacher adapter using the REUSE-14 validated BFS correction; no custom aggregation algorithm, optimizer, or expert rule engine was introduced. No hidden rule/task ID/grid/geometry entered the learned policy input (native 54 float32 partial image + direction features only). No held-out `hold_then_near` oracle label entered training.
- **Frozen design:** 3 cyclic train primitives, train seed base 7500, policy seeds [17,29], same independent evaluation seeds [9100,9101,9102] and 40-step episode caps; round0 DAgger beta=1 (expert actions executed), round1 beta=0 (learner actions executed, expert still queried every state). Nominal online lower-bound 256 interactions/model, min 128 real timesteps AND three episodes per round; 32 BC batch, four BC epochs per round. Both models actually completed TWO rounds.
- **Actual interaction and query budget:** EACH seed: **291 training environment actions**, **291 expert action-label queries**, 480 independent before-training evaluation actions, 480 independent after-training evaluation actions, **1,251 simulator actions per seed** counting training+evaluation. BOTH seeds: 582 train interactions, 582 expert queries, 1,920 before/after evaluation interactions, **2,502 total real simulator actions**. Whole DAgger collect+train runtime measured 5.503 seconds seed17 and 5.486 seconds seed29, excluding package install and the separate full evaluation time.
- **Label imbalance persisted in online dataset:** seed17 labels {0:130,1:69,2:64,3:22 pickup,4:6 put-down}; seed29 {0:132,1:83,2:49,3:21 pickup,4:6 put-down}. Both on-policy training rollouts included 26 episode resets (hold 9, near 9, putdown 8) and recorded 20 rewarded goal events (hold7, near7, putdown6), but many arise during beta=1 expert-controlled collection and **are not learned policy successes**. Different actual executed action distributions include unused native action5 (15 and17 steps), which expert never requested.
- **Independent closed-loop reward:** both seeds BEFORE and AFTER training achieved **hold 0/3, near 0/3, rewarded putdown 0/3, held-out chain 0/3**, 40-step cap on each of 12 evaluations per policy. In independent AFTER evaluations both models selected ONLY native actions 0/1 (forward/right), with NEVER action3 pickup or action4 putdown. Seed17 after evaluation aggregate by task hold {0:119,1:1}, near {0:119,1:1}, putdown {0:118,1:2}, heldout {0:119,1:1}; seed29 hold {0:114,1:6}, near {0:114,1:6}, putdown {0:118,1:2}, heldout {0:114,1:6}.
- **Scientific interpretation:** DAgger itself EXECUTED and added learner-state labels at beta=0, so this is a real integration of online corrective imitation, not just a test stub. Yet in the small 291-example-per-seed curriculum the plain BC training step again collapsed to frequent navigation actions, including only six put-down targets per seed; this is consistent with the known class imbalance from REUSE-9/10. It does not isolate effects of DAgger versus offline BC, because the supervised sampler was unbalanced, the expert data distribution and total interaction budgets changed, and no supervised per-action recall was recorded. NO acquired train task skill, NO evidence of transfer. Two rounds and small fixed tasks cannot establish DAgger failure.
- **Next discriminating gate, NOT another weight sweep:** on exactly the SAME DAgger-aggregated train-only observations/action labels, compare standard versus existing inverse-class-frequency `WeightedRandomSampler` BC under matched architecture, seeds and optimization sample exposures, no extra oracle queries during comparison, and still evaluate independent goal successes and rare-action recall. The current workflow saves only summary.json and deletes its temporary aggregation store, so this comparison would require a tightly scoped reproducible regeneration of online data with that additional trusted-data artifact. Avoid a larger DAgger step sweep, custom architectures, or attributing the zero to compositional transfer before an individual primitive succeeds. This isolates whether the known rare-action supervised collapse persists even in an on-policy expert-state distribution.

Is this mostly a class-imbalance supervised optimization failure EVEN WITH the collected online demonstration states, or do closed-loop execution/coverage problems persist after rare-action recall is recovered?

Existing heldout `hold_then_near` is never teacher labeled or collected, and does not enter any learner inputs. Both independent policies evaluated on seeds [9100,9101,9102], four tasks, 40-step cap, no privileged runtime features. No added teacher query between balanced and ordinary.

Report SHA256 per source pool, per-action label counts, supervision fit, action recalls, actual closed-loop primitive successes, heldout successes, and independently counted evaluation steps. Original REUSE-15 used ~291 interactions/seed but is NOT a matched comparator to the new replay fits; the only causal control here is ordinary vs balanced ON THE IDENTICAL per-seed source pool. Continue only if primitive competence becomes positive; if rare-action recall improves but all rewards stay at 0, treat off-policy execution/observability as the next unresolved research bottleneck rather than reweighting again.
- **Status:** source, narrow tests and dedicated run submitted; no measured inference before CI and artifact inspection.

- **Mechanistic observation:** balanced model seed17 issued 30 pickups and 83 putdowns across six independent putdown episodes but NEVER reached the rewarded placement goal; in its new-seed hold task it issued 3 pickups and 92 putdowns, with 3 terminal rewards. So rewarded putdown failure is not simply zero action4 selection. Balanced model seed29 often oscillated between turns on hold/near (108 right and108 left across each six-episode task), despite supervised pickup/putdown recall >95%/100% on original training observations. These are descriptive action-selection patterns, not validated causal explanations.
- **Interpretation:** matched class balancing yields measurable independently reproduced **partial source-skill competence** under one of two model seeds; new seed positives exceed the original single 9101 seed, strengthening the observation while remaining far from robust across policy seeds. Neither rewarded putdown nor causal composition has been learned. The critical scientific gap is now **multi-stage skill sequencing and deployment-state observability**, rather than a mere absence of rare action labels. A small falsifiable next test should reuse frozen policies to DIAGNOSE stage progression from simulator info (privileged for analysis only), including actually picked up/carrying/rewarded dropped transitions and action loops, versus matched ordinary learner. Do not add a new planner, oversized recurrent architecture, arbitrary reweighting sweep, or scale compute without this stage evidence. If later memory is studied, prefer established recurrent methods and information-matched controls.


### REUSE-18 preregistration — post-hoc causal phase diagnosis, not new reward validation

- **Research provenance:** REUSE-17 was already independently completed and logged by another GitHub-connected agent at \`73cacc714dc4bafcb8a8435b455e4c3aea2d8235\`. To avoid concurrency conflicts, the duplicate proposed REUSE-17 and overlapping log changes were both ABORTED without applying any commits. This experiment only adds the missing causal-stage diagnostic to the already-verified REUSE-17 work.
- **Frozen existing solution:** upstream XLand 0.9.3 simulator and \`imitation.BC\` 1.0.1; original safe numeric artifact from run \`39619756922\` with SHA-256 verified by existing \`load_frozen_numeric_pool\`. Two balanced learner model seeds 17/29, 12 epochs and 291 examples/seed with standard PyTorch \`WeightedRandomSampler\`. Original evaluation seeds 9100–9102 and six previously tested REUSE-17 seeds 9200–9205. This study explicitly REUSES these SAME observations and rewards; **not a new generalization test**. Assure exact observed reward seed sets reproduce REUSE-17 before interpreting events. No new expert queries, DAgger aggregation or training environment steps.
- **Question:** the frozen seed17 learner solves hold 4/9 and near 3/9 but rewarded putdown and heldout 0/9; seed29 is negative throughout. Does the learned policy actually pick up necessary tiles, trigger the near red-to-green rule, carry transformed goals, and successfully put down a previously carried tile? Distinguish merely selecting native action3/action4 from a real inventory transition and recognized placed goal.
- **Instrumentation:** \`policy.predict\` only receives upstream local image+orientation. Simulator state's pocket and grid may be inspected AFTER the policy chooses its action as privileged EVALUATION diagnostics only. Per episode report first and aggregate counts for effective pickups/putdowns, carrying blue-square/red-ball/green-ball, newly transformed green tile on grid and blue-square near red ball, actual reward, action counts and cap. These metrics identify bottleneck phases, but do not prove causal rule induction or generalizable transfer.
- **Stop rule:** If no effective drop follows frequent action4 choices, prioritize diagnosing action preconditions and phase-aware navigation; if pickup/drop occur but no TileNearGoal, isolate successful spatial placement. No new learning method, new weight sweep, heldout-chain teacher labels or architecture scaling permitted by this checkpoint alone.
- **Status:** implementation submitted; no measured phase evidence asserted until GitHub workflow artifact.


### REUSE-18 VERIFIED — actual causal-stage transitions explain the frozen putdown failure

- **Source, successful workflow and artifact:** commit `280b42cb62eb75694693a23118348001a2a4ff2e` developed a posthoc milestone-only analysis using the exact original REUSE-16 numeric expert pools and the independent REUSE-17 replay policy seeds. Dedicated GitHub run `39622825399` finished SUCCESS; artifact `bagi-frozen-causal-phases` ID `12355312869`, `summary.json` downloaded and inspected. Both model seeds reproduced REUSE-17's precise original and six-new evaluation successful-seed sets before their phase telemetry was accepted. No new DAgger collection, teacher labels, heldout oracle calls or privileged learned-policy inputs.
- **Full measured accounting:** 2,711 real simulator steps across both balanced policy seeds, four tasks, and nine already-tested evaluation seeds per task. This is a **posthoc explanation of previous success-rate observations**, NOT a new independent generalization sample. Evaluator inspects XLand grid/pocket before/after the policy-selected action strictly for diagnostics. The learned BC policy sees only local image+heading.
- **Six newer seeds, seed17 actual causal progress:** hold **3/6** goals, each corresponding to **3/6** genuine effective pickups and carrying a red-ball goal. Near **2/6** goals, both showed actual red→green map transformations (**2/6**), effective pickup (**2/6**) and carrying the resulting green-ball goal (**2/6**). Putdown **0/6** goals; only **1/6** effective pickup of the desired blue square, **0/6** effective putdowns, **0/6** observed correctly placed blue-square-next-to-red arrangement, even though this learned policy selected action4 (put-down) **83 times** across these 6 episodes. In the single fresh putdown episode where it carried blue square, it instead issued 30 action3 pickup attempts and no action4.
- **Six newer seeds, seed29:** no heldout/hold/near/putdown reward. Hold and near each **0/6** effective pickups or green transformation. On putdown task, seed29 produced **2/6** effective pickups (one grabbed a wrong red ball), **1/6** actual blue-square-carrying episode, **0/6** effective putdowns and **0/6** properly placed blue-next-to-red tiles. It selected action4 **100 times** across the six putdown episodes, yet did not effectively drop a carried object. This rejects a simple 'no putdown action ever chosen' explanation and supports a stronger action-precondition/phase-sequencing failure.
- **Scientific conclusion:** Existing sampler class balancing has supported a modest learned terminal skill for hold/near in one model seed, including a genuine intermediate red→green transformation. A training-label action4 recall of 100% does not imply executing the causal placement stage; the learner often presses putdown while not carrying the needed object, or presses pickup repeatedly after carrying it. This is evidence for failed closed-loop control/phase recognition (with task information hidden), not a proof of any particular representational deficiency or need for a custom architecture. No heldout causal composition is learned.
- **Engineering validation:** dedicated experiment lint/unit tests and simulator replay passed; two default repository CI jobs `39622825186` and `39622834847` failed because the default `pip install -e .` environment lacks optional `xminigrid` while two new unit tests imported `xminigrid` directly. Fix those tests using `pytest.importorskip("xminigrid")` within the phase-specific cases; the dedicated workflow already installs and runs their real upstream tile semantics. CI breakage is orthogonal to the measured science.
- **Next meaningful gate:** before expensive recurrent/model scaling or further class-sampling sweeps, test existing information-matched stateful baselines with a correct actuator history (the 4-action memory lookups in REUSE-12 were expert-trajectory information, not deployed belief learning). First confirm DAgger's beta=0 collection records the actual executed actions and episode boundaries, because its stored transitions contain the TEACHER label even when the learner executed something else. A history-based learner trained on those mislabeled as executed actions would have information leakage/invalid causal history. Alternatively use existing XLand recurrent PPO with a properly specified task-observation boundary on a vetted small training-skill gate. Do not claim robust generalization or train on the frozen heldout chain.


### REUSE-19 preregistration — true executed-action DAgger provenance, before learner memory

- **Scientific motivation and evidence:** REUSE-18 (successful dedicated \`39623603317\` and both CI checks) found phase-dependent failure on rewarded putdown despite action4 often being selected. REUSE-12 found a 0/1/4 PREVIOUS-EXPERT-ACTION majority lookup increase on on-oracle training trajectories, but this is not a valid proxy for the learner's own past action history during deployment. Upstream HumanCompatibleAI \`imitation.algorithms.dagger.InteractiveTrajectoryCollector\` v1.0.1 documentation/source explicitly preserves the CURRENT EXPERT action as the label in saved \`Trajectory\` objects even when beta=0 injects a different learned-policy action into the underlying environment. Mistaking stored labels for actually executed actions would leak incorrect past-action information.
- **Build-vs-reuse decision:** reuse upstream \`SimpleDAggerTrainer\`, \`LinearBetaSchedule(1)\`, \`BC\`, the original REUSE-15 training-only XLand bridge and REUSE-14 corrective teacher unchanged. Introduce only a narrow callback wrapper to record the label offered to DAgger and actual action received by the simulator. No new algorithm, architecture, learning objective or oracle rules.
- **Frozen falsifiable audit:** policy seeds [17,29], training-only hold, near and rewarded putdown cycling, seed base 7500, 40-action cap, same 2-round beta schedule 1.0 expert-executed then 0.0 learner-executed, 256 minimum training steps, min 128 actions/round and 3 episodes/round, original 4 BC epochs and 32 batch. 400 actual online step upper gate per seed, every expert query and simulator action counted. Abort if label callback and simulator-step logs are not 1-to-1 or if the DAgger saved expert labels differ from the logged state/label multiset. A beta=1 round must show zero expert-execution disagreement; beta=0 mismatch is the object of measurement.
- **History gate:** save only numeric current local observation+heading (54 float32 features), expert label, actual native executed action, 4 PRECEDING executed actions with padding action-ID 6 and reset at episode boundaries, round, episode ID, step index and terminal flags. The current label cannot appear inside its own history. On full and beta=0-only samples, compare 0/1/4 preceding ACTUAL actions for exact teacher-label contradictory signatures and in-sample majority lookup, and contrast to 4 preceding expert labels as an explicitly invalid deployed-policy counterfactual. More distinct signatures trivially make in-sample lookup fit better: these lookup ceilings are NOT policy generalization and must not be called proof of memory success.
- **Safety/resource:** no heldout chain training state, oracle label or runtime privileged learner feature; only original three train primitives. No independent heldout test, as no new learned model is proposed here. Saved numeric NPZ uses no object dtype and can be safely loaded with \`allow_pickle=False\`. This online provenance collection repeats the smallest previous online DAgger run solely because REUSE-16 saved expert labels but NOT the actual actions that caused visited observations. Do not enlarge training budget or launch a recurrent learner until this audit confirms correct action-stream identity.
- **Status:** submitted to dedicated GitHub source+workflow; no findings asserted before measured artifact is inspected.


### REUSE-19 VERIFIED — real executed action histories and teacher labels diverge in beta=0

- **Verified provenance:** corrected DAgger audit run \`39637067506\` at commit \`755c0ffb05b1cbf82f76bd9fda38fd6de4c31211\`, artifact \`bagi-dagger-action-provenance\` ID \`12362840074\` downloaded and inspected (\`summary.json\`, numeric-only per-seed \`action_provenance_seed17.npz\` and \`action_provenance_seed29.npz\`). Both head CI checks \`39637067560\` and \`39637074305\` completed successfully. Earlier source commits had two engineering/test failures: bad closure late binding lint then mismatched raw observation byte dtypes after upstream trajectory serialization. REUSE-19 now validates upstream numeric *canonical float32* observation+label multisets exactly, preserving strict pair matching; no scientific outcome inferred from failed runs.
- **Source:** upstream \`SimpleDAggerTrainer\` 1.0.1, \`LinearBetaSchedule(1)\`, original REUSE-15 XLand wrapper and REUSE-14 corrective expert. Only changes are action execution logging, episode-boundary resets and provenance checks. Teacher's labels are recorded before real XLand step and matched 1:1 against 291 upstream DAgger labels per seed. No heldout oracle; no privileged policy inputs.
- **True simulator and label accounting:** both policy seeds 17/29 each had **291 actual simulator actions and 291 training-only expert label queries** (26 episode resets across 3 training tasks). Overall **582 actual steps and 582 expert labels**. Round0 beta=1: each seed **131 simulator steps**, exactly 0 teacher/executed action mismatches and 20 rewarded completions, all expert-guided (NOT learner successes). Round1 beta=0: each seed **160 simulator steps**, **zero** rewarded completions. Seed17 teacher/executed mismatches **133/160 = 83.125%**, seed29 **126/160 = 78.75%**. Seed17 round1 teacher actions {0:55,1:55,2:48,3:2,4:0}, while ACTUAL executed actions {0:35,1:28,2:29,3:28,4:25,5:15}; seed29 round1 teacher labels {0:57,1:69,2:33,3:1,4:0}, ACTUAL actions {0:41,1:30,2:22,3:32,4:18,5:17}. Critical: neither learner-driven round visited a state where the expert preferred a putdown action, despite frequent incorrect action4 execution.
- **Exact in-sample action-history ambiguity on beta=0 visits:** seed17 local observation alone: 48 distinct signatures, 1 conflicting group, 2 minority-label errors (majority in-sample fit 158/160). 1 preceding actually executed action: 118 signatures, 1 conflicting group, 1 error; 4 preceding ACTUAL actions: 159 signatures, 0 conflicts/0 errors. Seed29 current view: 57 signatures, 2 conflicts, 4 errors (156/160); 1 actual past action: 125 signatures, 2 conflicts, 3 errors; 4 actual actions: 159 signatures, 0 conflicts/0 errors. Each four-history case has ONLY ONE repeated signature group on 160 rows; 100% exact majority fit is therefore **singleton inflation and NOT a generalization result**. Counterfactual four preceding EXPERT labels also had zero conflicts but would be incorrect deployment-history information when beta=0. The saved numeric pools include ONLY actual preceding four actions, current local learner observation and current label separately.
- **Interpretation:** the required action-history provenance issue is scientifically real, not a training effect: DAgger's \`Trajectory.acts\` are expert target labels, not actions actually executed during learner-driven rollouts, as stated by upstream code. Using them to construct learner history would misrepresent past actions for ~79–83% of decisions. This work prevented an invalid recurrent imitation experiment. In addition, the expert never recommends putdown on learner-driven states, indicating the on-policy distribution is stuck before the placement precondition. No new learner was trained or independently evaluated for reward here.
- **Next reuse-first gate:** inspect whether BAGI's task-agnostic 54-feature \`img+direction\` policy omitted a STANDARD UPSTREAM task-instruction input before attempting recurrent architectures. Upstream \`xminigrid.wrappers.RulesAndGoalsObservationWrapper\` explicitly adds \`goal_encoding\` and \`rule_encoding\` (source \`dunnolab/xland-minigrid/src/xminigrid/wrappers.py\`). Original BAGI reused only \`DirectionObservationWrapper\` so its agent never directly observed rule/goal encoding. A tiny reset-only audit must show which task differences are hidden for the SAME initial layout and distinguish an instruction-conditioned benchmark from the old sensory-only benchmark. This is a scientifically important information-budget confound, not permission to leak full map into policy or train on heldout demonstrations.

### REUSE-20 preregistration — upstream XLand rules/goals observation interface boundary

- **Reused solution:** upstream \`xminigrid==0.9.3\` \`RulesAndGoalsObservationWrapper\` composes with \`DirectionObservationWrapper\` and makes \`goal_encoding\` and \`rule_encoding\` part of the declared agent observation. This is documented by upstream maintained code, not a BAGI invention. Compare to existing BAGI \`visible_features\` which concatenates ONLY local image and direction (54 features). The existing previous memoryless learner never observed upstream task encodings or a task identifier. Learning a variable causal rule without task conditioning may be underdetermined; do not assume that this proves learning is impossible.
- **Question/test:** on 3 fixed independent reset seeds [9300,9301,9302], inspect three original training rulesets (hold,near,putdown) and frozen heldout goal/rule SHAPES and SHA256 metadata ONLY. Compare the same seed and source object initialization for hold versus heldout composition: identical physical initial grid/agent and prior image+direction? Are the available upstream goals/rules different and exposed ONLY by the instruction wrapper? Assert wrapper preserves physical state and local features exactly. No teacher oracle, reward sampling, policy training, or heldout trajectory/simulation actions. 4 tasks *3 seeds *2 wrappers = 24 real environment RESET calls; zero simulator STEPS.
- **Scientific stop rule:** if upstream task instructions are valid visible input and distinguish otherwise identical task starts, then an information-matched standard BC comparison with/without encoded rules+goal becomes a more relevant next basic test than an arbitrary recurrent architecture; but this would change the task information budget, require fixed-size rule padding/masking across varying numbers of rules and avoid heldout training labels. If prior observation already contains all relevant instructions, do not add redundant features. A reset-only interface audit provides no learned-skill result and cannot claim causal transfer.
- **Security/splits:** heldout goal and rule are inspected for one narrow *metadata-only* information-boundary contrast; neither are used in learner training, teacher queries, demonstrations, reward evaluation or hyperparameter selection. Never expose privileged grid geometry to a learner. Preserve independent training/evaluation split and cost. No new algorithms are justified by this audit.
- **Status:** submitted with dedicated verification workflow; results unmeasured until audited artifact.

### REUSE-21 engineering clarification — 1.0.1 BC requires transition-shaped batches

### REUSE-21 VERIFIED — upstream rules/goals yield modest independent training-skill gains

- **Evidence:** dedicated GitHub experiment run `39639959628` at corrected source commit `26a93c119a803596a09a0ba9693895cd0d790e51` COMPLETED SUCCESS; archived artifact `bagi-upstream-instruction-bc`, ID `12364777155`, was downloaded and its `summary.json` inspected. Both corrected-source CI checks `39639959657` and `39639963654` passed. Earlier runs at `1406c5d` and `b2d9f87` failed import-lint and pinned `imitation` transition-collator compatibility before obtaining a learning result; do not interpret them as experimental outcomes.
- **Upstream methods and input boundary:** unchanged `imitation.BC` v1.0.1, upstream `torch.utils.data.WeightedRandomSampler`, XLand v0.9.3 and its PUBLIC `RulesAndGoalsObservationWrapper`. Verified REUSE-19 training-only current observations and expert labels were reused without further teacher queries: 291 source examples per policy seed, 3492 optimization sample exposures/model, matching seeds [17,29], original feedforward BC architecture with 87 inputs. Compare SHAM (54 local image+heading + 33 constant slots) versus INSTRUCTED (same 54 inputs + goal length5 + four padded rules x7). Source-task identity is recovered from prior TRAIN-only cyclic episode indices. No full map, hidden pocket, heldout expert label or causal-chain training sequence enters policy.
- **Engineering caveat:** pinned imitation's `transitions_collate_fn` needs `next_obs/dones` fields despite the supervised BC loss using only `obs/acts`. Source provenance did not record true terminal successor observation; the explicit `next_obs=obs.copy()` placeholder is NONPHYSICAL and used only for compatibility with the upstream collator. It is NOT a claimed transition or learned dynamics, is never read by the BC supervised loss, and does not enter reward evaluation.
- **Real closed-loop experiment:** five previously unseen layout seeds [9500,9501,9502,9503,9504] across training hold/near/putdown and frozen heldout chain, 40 action cap. **2935 total real simulator evaluation actions**, zero additional training environment actions and **zero new teacher queries**. Seed17 SHAM hold 1/5 (seed9501), near 0/5, putdown 0/5, heldout 0/5; seed17 INSTRUCTED hold 1/5 (9501), near 0/5, putdown 0/5, heldout 0/5. Seed29 SHAM hold 1/5 (9501), near 0/5, putdown 0/5, heldout 0/5; seed29 INSTRUCTED hold **3/5** (9500,9501,9502), near **1/5** (9502), putdown 0/5 and heldout 0/5.
- **Aggregated matched pair:** SHAM hold **2/10** vs INSTRUCTED **4/10**; SHAM near **0/10** vs INSTRUCTED **1/10**; both putdown **0/10** and both composition **0/10**. Source expert action3/4 recall remained high in these same-data fits but did not predict rewarded placement. Instructed seed29 training accuracy was 57.04% versus 48.45% sham; seed17 instructed 50.86% versus 52.58% sham. This small comparison supports a limited causal effect of public instructions on some primitive rollouts, NOT robust acquisition, transfer, or statistically established benefit.
- **Research interpretation:** earlier 54-feature policies lacked a standard upstream task-instruction input; this 87-feature study CHANGES the information budget relative to those earlier studies. Within REUSE-21 the width, initial seed, source data, sampler and optimization exposures were held fixed, so only the instruction-information slots varied. A source-skill gate is still not reliably passed for rewarded putdown or across both model seeds; no causal composition is learned.
- **Next justified unknown:** isolate whether XLand policy observations include pocket/inventory/action-precondition feedback and whether authentic executed-action history suffices to track post-pickup state. Prefer an upstream-supported observation/stateful baseline with matched controls over new architectures, arbitrary tuning or increased DAgger compute. Do not train on heldout chain teacher examples.


### REUSE-22 preregistration — upstream inventory observability and causal action-precondition alias

- **Evidence motivating test:** REUSE-21 successful pinned BC experiment \`39639959628\` with public upstream goal+rule tokens (87 input features) showed at most modest independent training hold 4/10 and near 1/10, but 0/10 rewarded putdown and 0/10 heldout chain; same-width sham hold 2/10 and near 0/10. Exact data and learning counts are in the verified preceding entry. The source CI and dedicated experiment passed. Earlier REUSE-18 stage traces established frequent selected putdown actions while no effective drop occurs. Need isolate standard observation semantics, not tune another BC model blindly.
- **Upstream implementation inspected:** \`xminigrid.core.observation.transparent_field_of_view\` deliberately contains a COMMENTED OUT line \`fov_grid = fov_grid.at[height - 1, width // 2].set(agent.pocket)\` directly under an explanatory note about observing the pocket. Therefore even the upstream \`RulesAndGoalsObservationWrapper\` and \`DirectionObservationWrapper\` add no pocket/inventory field. The same observation may be emitted for states with different pockets. This observation-boundary issue is not an implementation error by BAGI, and preserving upstream benchmark semantics matters.
- **Bounded falsifiable native-simulator audit:** for each predeclared TRAIN putdown source layout seed [7300,7301,7302], reuse the existing verified rewarded putdown BFS oracle to reach the REAL physical pre-drop state and record replay steps. At each, create ONE explicit COUNTERFACTUAL copy with only \`agent.pocket\` edited to upstream EMPTY token while preserving grid, position, direction, task goal and rule. Recompute official \`transparent_field_of_view\` and require exact image identity, original direction and public instruction equality across the pair. Apply native action4 put-down separately to both timesteps, requiring real rewarded drop vs no counterfactual reward and different resulting grid. Log per seed actual generator, prefix replay and counterfactual simulator calls and exact input hashes.
- **Research constraints:** edited-pocket counterfactual is NOT claimed simulator-reachable; do not use it as proof that a recurrent learner cannot infer inventory from legal action history. Its purpose is to show that CURRENT local+task-conditioned observation contains no direct feedback about a causally relevant state variable. Privileged full grid/pocket ONLY in diagnostic replay, not learner input. No new fitted model, heldout-chain actions, teacher labels, or custom learning architecture. If positive, next experiment may compare existing upstream-supplied public instructions + true learner action history to a same-width sham context, not leak state or increase training by guessing.
- **Status:** preregistered source, tests and dedicated workflow submitted; results unmeasured until real simulator evidence is archived.


### REUSE-22 VERIFIED — missing upstream inventory field is a causal-control observation bottleneck

- **Provenance:** source commit `306a61dc38fa4a2c1ba9ca779c9b1163ebabed63`, dedicated run `39643131034` SUCCESS, artifact `bagi-inventory-observation-aliasing` ID `12365782326` downloaded and `summary.json` inspected. Real existing training-only putdown expert generated successful pre-drop states on seeds [7300,7301,7302] in respectively 9/17/8 actions; 34 oracle generator simulator steps, 31 real prefix replay steps and 6 paired native-putdown step calls: **71 actual simulator step calls**, 0 new learned models, 0 heldout teacher labels.
- **Native causality audit:** original pre-drop pockets held the required blue square. Creating explicitly COUNTERFACTUAL copies editing ONLY that hidden pocket to upstream EMPTY preserves image, direction, goal and rule-encoding fields **exactly on all three cases**. The upstream image was independently recomputed via its original `transparent_field_of_view` and matched full-state observation. Same native action4 then yielded reward 0.7975 / 0.6175 / 0.82 in physical real pocket states but **0** in all three counterfactual empty-pocket variants; different resulting grids and object placement. This proves the benchmark's upstream task-conditioned instantaneous observation omits a native-action-relevant state variable.
- **Critical limitation:** the altered pocket-only states were intentionally **not claimed reachable** via valid XLand transitions. The observation alias finding is a counterfactual input-precondition fact, not a proof that recurrent agents cannot infer inventory from legal action/action-observation histories. The successful physical states were reached via existing expert planner, and no full simulator state was supplied to any learned agent.
- **Implication:** source-skill putdown failure after REUSE-21 may involve both missing pocket feedback and learned action-history phase inference. Next do a tightly matched information-control test on verified real executed-action histories before inventing new architectures or leaking a hidden pocket feature directly.

### REUSE-23 preregistered — public instructions plus true execution history vs same-width sham

- **Reuse rather than build:** original `imitation.BC` 1.0.1 and `torch.WeightedRandomSampler`, original XLand `RulesAndGoalsObservationWrapper` 0.9.3, and prior verified `REUSE-19` safe numeric expert/ACTUAL executed history artifact. Both feedforward BC policies have exactly 115 input features: 54 local img+heading + 33 upstream public goal/rule tokens + four seven-way one-hot history slots (28). Identical source label pools 291/seed, same balanced sample indices, seeds [17,29], architecture and 12 epochs (3,492 sample exposures/policy). **Only difference:** four preceding actual learner actions or four constant PAD values in the same slots. At inference, history updates with learner's OWN actually executed actions, never teacher labels; source history re-derived and checked at episode boundaries. No new expert labels or online training environment interactions.
- **Frozen evaluation:** five previously untested layout seeds [9600,9601,9602,9603,9604], four tasks (3 training primitives + frozen heldout chain), 40-step cap. Report reward successes, real evaluation simulator steps and rare action supervised fit separately. Heldout task never demonstrated, teacher queried, or used in source pool. All learned policies see only official upstream public instruction fields and local tiles/direction plus own actions. No privileged pocket/map features.
- **Interpretation gate:** if adding real history improves rewarded source skills over the information-matched sham, it supports the value of policy-side action feedback under fixed instruction availability but does not prove causal transfer. If both fail rewarded putdown or only supervised recalls improve, the four-step window may be too short for pickup-to-drop memory; do not launch large recurrent agents until stage traces justify it. This finite two-model, five-seed study is not a population benchmark.
- **Status:** submitted source and dedicated workflow; no measured REUSE-23 outcomes before CI and artifact inspection.


### REUSE-23 VERIFIED — first learned putdown reward; real history does not change reward counts

- **Source and CI:** experimental commit f3b1523611967a94dfb454ebb12bee69a85aa570, dedicated run 39644180240 SUCCESS, artifact bagi-instruction-actual-history-control ID 12366427664 downloaded and summary.json inspected. Both source CI runs 39644180222 and 39644187922 passed. Earlier draft commit 3c92b59 failed unused-import lint BEFORE training; not an experiment outcome.

Seed17 source SHA256 f893dc633a14bb9347c5e12a2ac5799f450b49a2daa700e4e67845f2176dd4ba; seed29 source SHA256 0735b160ef62a353b3fb52dd8df496630ffe5104ec10d24b1bfb2b22a9b83675. Source identity verified for BOTH conditions. Exactly 291 labeled examples/seed, balanced sampled 144 distinct rows seed17 and 147 seed29; 12 epochs and 3492 sample exposures per model. Both 115-wide BC networks see identical local 54 + upstream PUBLIC task instructions 33 + action history 28 inputs, except genuine preceding four EXECUTED actions vs constant PAD slots, with identical policy initialization seeds 17/29. At inference genuine history uses ONLY own actual native actions with episode resets; never preceding expert labels.
- **Frozen new test:** eval seeds 9600,9601,9602,9603,9604, three training primitives plus heldout causal chain, 40-action cap, exactly 2684 actual evaluation simulator steps across two seeds and two conditions, ZERO new expert label queries or online training simulator actions, zero heldout examples, zero privileged policy state. Seed17 sham AND real-action history: hold 2/5 [9600,9604], near 1/5 [9600], putdown 0/5, heldout 0/5. Seed29 sham AND real history: hold 2/5 [9600,9604], near 1/5 [9600], putdown 1/5 [9604], heldout 0/5. Thus per condition hold 4/10, near 2/10, putdown 1/10, heldout 0/10; precise rewarded seed IDs matched between controls.

All four putdown recall values 100% on training source labels, but only ONE independent rewarded putdown. The putdown reward under seed29/layout9604 is the FIRST verified learner-controlled BC putdown success in this experiment series, NOT a positive causal effect of added four-action history: sham and history each succeeded on exactly the same layout. Their action distributions can differ, even when goal rewards agree.
- **Limits and next gate:** this small paired test does not show that action history is universally useless; four actions may not bridge long pickup-to-drop navigation and hidden inventory is empirically confirmed by REUSE-22. No robust putdown or heldout causal composition. Next bounded research check: replicate the now-frozen two matched policies on fresh previously unexamined layout seeds with no added training or oracle data, and report true effective pickups/drops and exact success seed IDs. Avoid architecture growth, arbitrary sampler tuning, or treating rare supervised recall as learned multi-stage competence.


### REUSE-24 preregistration — untouched-layout replication and stage attribution

Source putdown reward 1/10/condition is not reliable and cannot justify more sampler/model hyperparameter sweeps. REUSE-22 also proved upstream instant observation hides carried inventory. The next checkpoint is frozen-policy independent-layout replication with stage evidence, not a proposed new algorithm.
- **Methods/source:** exact original REUSE-23 `imitation.BC` 1.0.1, `WeightedRandomSampler`, upstream XLand-MiniGrid 0.9.3 PUBLIC rule/goal observation, verified REUSE-19 numeric labels; no oracle/DAgger acquisition. Two same-network-width (115) conditions retain four true preceding ACTUAL executed actions vs equal-width constant PAD context. Seeds17/29, 291 training-only rows per seed, 12 epochs, 3492 sampled row exposures per condition, 40-step episodes. Source numeric SHA256 integrity rechecked. REUSE-23 archived original summary downloaded from run39644180240, all exact original reward-success layout IDs on seeds9600..9604 must reproduce before reporting new-layout outcomes.
- **Primary evaluation:** precommitted ten previously unseen layout seeds9700..9709, ONLY the three training primitive tasks hold, near, putdown. No heldout-chain evaluation or training since source putdown gate is weak. Report per condition and model seed actual independent reward success, per-layout action counts and true simulator steps, without calling any teacher. Record whether each trajectory exhibits an effective native-action pickup (pocket changed from empty to nonempty), effective drop (pocket changed from full to empty), observed held square/red/green, new green-rule transformation, square adjacent to red, and pickup-to-drop action gaps; these privileged state variables are read only AFTER policy action choice for diagnostic accounting, NEVER provided to the learner.

If true action-history does not increase fresh successes beyond sham, do not conclude that full recurrent memory is useless: four previous actions can expire during long navigation. Reuse maintained upstream XLand recurrent PPO or SB3-Contrib RecurrentPPO as the next independently budgeted benchmark candidate rather than inventing an RNN and more BC ablations. Compare legal upstream instructions and the same training task/interaction budget. No compositional transfer claims until primitives become independently reliable. Report successes even if all zero.

- **Published foundations:** Kaelbling/Littman/Cassandra on partially observed belief (1998); Sutton/Precup/Singh on temporal abstraction/options (1999); Hafner et al. DreamerV3 on learned dynamics (2025); Barreto et al. successor features/transfer (2018); Wang et al. Voyager on accumulating verified skills (2024); Ross et al. DAgger (2011). Existing BAGI DQN, Workshop, world model, MPC and published XLand learner methods are preserved.
- **Implemented G0:** general goal/option controller in bagi/agents/general_brain.py: observable Goal facts, pluggable Skill policies with int or symbolic command actions, a strict public-observation-only sensor interface, persistent confirmed/retracted facts, bounded goal-conditioned option search, verification of executed-action outcomes (not assumed effects/reward), capped retries, simple cross-goal Bayesian skill-success statistics. NO BLUE, XLand, Workshop or application-specific branches in the core. docs/GENERAL_AGENT_ARCHITECTURE.md covers research basis, interfaces, invariants and preregistered G1/G2/G3 gates. The multi-task smoke in bagi/experiments/general_brain_smoke.py and tests/test_general_brain.py exercise the same core on unrelated synthetic supply-chain and software-workflow tasks and a failed option with transferred learned reliability.

This does NOT demonstrate learning vision, automatic option discovery, robust transfer in real environments, autonomous physical manipulation or AGI. NEW real Workshop native steps=0, real XLand native steps=0, oracle labels=0, autonomous BLUE successes=0. Skills and verified symbolic facts are supplied by synthetic adapters; in real POMDPs visual effect extraction still must be learned or separately justified.
- **Next falsifiable gate:** real public-only Workshop plus XLand adapters reusing existing learned policies, matched native step caps, never-before-used layouts/goals, reaction-only vs unverified-action-latch vs verified-outcome/replanning controls, physical pickup/drop/adjacency/reward independent of vacuous score, hold/near retention and calibration of false-positive effect detections. ### G2-D1 preregistered root-cause fault-localization (2026-10-25)

- **Question:** Why do the existing learned policies fail most unfamiliar goals? Prior REUSE-37 expanded source had real BLUE pickup in only 6/36 independent eligible putdown episodes, physical placement 5/36; G1 direct learner physically completed 4/10 versus option fallback 3/10, which did not establish a cause or prove a planner fix. Need identify causal bottleneck before another algorithm, not hardcode BLUE behavior in GeneralBrain.
- **ONE preregistered intervention** on SAME frozen source-verified upstream `imitation.BC` expanded models seed17/29: real last-four executed-action history vs precisely their final28 public onehot action-history features replaced with PAD on policy input only. Image, heading, public rules/goal, model weights, model seed and original 787 sampled row/12-epoch supervised recipe identical. This is an OUT-OF-DISTRIBUTION history erasure diagnostic, **not** a matched-trained memoryless model and not evidence alone that recurrent policies work/fail. True executed history always advances for both arms.
- **Mechanism measurement:** on each putdown learner-state, AFTER the learned policy has already selected its own action, run a separate single native XLand pickup action3 on a DISCARDED branch whenever the learner did not choose pickup and it has never physically acquired BLUE. A counterfactual evaluator-only BLUE grid-to-pocket transition measures a feasible pickup opportunity. This branch never influences the acting learner, its subsequent states, rewards, labels or training; count ALL hypothetical native calls separately. Evaluator-only inspection of grid/pocket never goes to policy. Also record BLUE visibility in public image, opportunities, missed pickups, ineffective pickups/wrong object, genuine pickup/drop, adjacency, reward and first-failure stage; do not conflate these descriptive buckets with proven causes.
- **Fixed new untouched layouts** 11500–11509 for upstream tasks hold, near, nonadjacent putdown, no replacement for ineligible seeds, 2 model seeds ×2 policy-input conditions ×3 tasks ×10 layouts=120 episodes, cap40 actual actions = <=4,800; <=1,600 additional discarded putdown counterfactuals, <=6,400 TOTAL actual native calls. ZERO teacher queries or learner TRAIN environment interactions. Frozen source numeric hashes, matched sampled training provenance and no composition teacher access enforced by existing G1 source model reconstruction. Both task reward retention and TRUE physical BLUE pickup -> drop -> adjacent + native reward measured, with numerator/eligible denominator. Source-only 9/9 local synthetic action-history and causal-isolation tests pass; not native successes.
- **Decision after artifact:** opportunity almost never appears => active sensing/navigation; opportunity appears but pickup not taken => initiation/action-selection; pickup succeeds but drop not => outcome belief/termination; history ablation changes matched results => history sensitivity. Null or mixed results require more targeted test, not architecture claims. G2 follow-up should validate observation-only learned effect estimates on episode-disjoint Workshop and XLand; no hardcoded benchmark-specific controls in the general brain.

### G2-E1 public-action effect prediction preregistration (2026-10-25)

- **Existing work and specific gap:** G2-D3/D4 native artifacts and current `PublicStagnationEscape` verify reduced action repetition and a small paired physical benefit (9/56 direct vs 13/56 escape), but unchanged public observation is not a verified physical no-op and no learned action-effect predictor exists. No terminated goal-reaching action and no native reward1 in the entire 7,296-action archive. This fails all preregistered training, default and mirror learned-competence thresholds; zero reward cannot be reinterpreted as goal success or transfer.
- **Causal-stage diagnostics, evaluator-only:** TRAIN seed17 switch28/key1/lock0/goal0; seed29 switch33/key2/lock0/goal0. Heldout default seed17 learned switch1 vs random9 (random key1); seed29 learned switch0 vs random7. Heldout mirror seed17 learned switch0 vs random10; seed29 learned switch1 vs random10 (random key1). No lock or goal events in any arm. Learned TEST policies collapsed to repetitive actions: default seed17 **398/400 LEFT**, default seed29 **360/400 DOWN**; each learned TEST arm only **6 unique public trajectories /10 starts**, versus random **10/10**. Action collapse and sparse-reward coverage are plausible contributors, not established unique causal explanations. Ten starts share a fixed geometry; mirror_x is one geometry, NOT broad layout transfer.
- **Decision / reuse:** G2-E2-P0 is a negative **feasibility** result for this exact reused DQN configuration and budget, NOT evidence that DQN, memory or general learning are impossible. Respect the preregistered **STOP**: no heldout-driven retuning, extra seeds, new training budget, duplicate workflow or unearned learned-Workshop-competence claim. The G2-E1 predictor also failed its action-frequency gate; consequently no learned two-domain action-effect or continual-transfer claim is promoted. Next bounded work should use existing TRAIN-only trajectories to diagnose reward/event coverage and policy collapse, then preregister a genuinely distinct reuse-first sparse-reward/partial-observation intervention with fresh heldout conditions and matched real-action controls; avoid task-specific scripted stage logic. No new simulator actions, teacher queries or model updates were performed during this independent artifact audit.
